#2916. 13 young Eagles worth watching in what's left of a lost season

13 young Eagles worth watching in what's left of a lost season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles aren't even at the halfway point yet.

There are still nine games to go in this miserable 2026 season. And now that the Eagles are sitting at 2-6 with a point differential of -65 it probably feels a little daunting to have to watch this team play nine more times.

Maybe some Eagles fans really will do something else on Sundays — not to mention Thanksgiving and Christmas Eve — but let's be honest: Most of you are still going to watch when the Eagles play.

So other than rooting for a good draft pick, how do you find enjoyment out of a lost season like this? The answer is pretty easy. You get to monitor the development of the young players on the team. And, hopefully, you'll be able to see their progress in the second half of this season.

With that in mind, here are 13 young Eagles who are worth watching the rest of the way:

QB Joe Milton: Maybe the Eagles really do just ride it out with backup quarterback Tanner McKee in place of Jalen Hurts. But there's some definite intrigue with Milton. The Eagles signed him off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16 and it didn't take long for Milton to supplant Cole Payton as the new backup. The 26-year-old Milton (6-5, 236) has played in just five career games but the former sixth-round pick has plenty of arm talent. His highlight reel from his 2023 season at Tennessee is littered with highlight plays. Milton has a big arm and can elude pressure. He just wasn't very consistent. But in this lost season, if McKee continues to struggle, it would be fun to see Milton get a chance.

QB Cole Payton: The Eagles on Sunday night made an interesting decision. They kept Cole Payton active over offensive lineman Jake Majors but Payton didn't play. My first thought was that Payton would have a role on special teams but he didn't. So perhaps there was an offensive role in mind for Payton that the Eagles didn't get to in their 27-10 loss — or maybe he was just an emergency RB option. Payton is a heck of an athlete and while he's developing as a QB behind the scenes, maybe there's a chance to get him on the field in some other roles.

RB Jaydon Blue: In the loss to the Commanders, Blue played just 8 snaps on offense and 7 snaps on special teams, despite Saquon Barkley being banged up and others missing. Blue 22, was a fifth-round pick out of Texas last year but played in just five games with the Cowboys as a rookie. Blue has an intriguing skillset. He ran a 4.38 at the Combine last year and also showed an ability as a receiving threat at Texas. He's a different kind of back and it would be fun to see him get some burn.

WR Makai Lemon: The first-round pick from USC got off to a slow start in his rookie season but has been getting better and better once the Eagles scrapped the portion of the playbook that had him going backwards. Over the last couple of weeks without DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks, Lemon has played more and has had his two biggest games of the season. Overall, Lemon has 31 catches on 45 targets for 251 yards and a touchdown. Among rookies, Lemon is sixth in receiving yards behind Carnell Tate, Denzel Boston, KC Concepcion, Germie Bernard and Antonio Williams. But Lemon has been coming on stronger recently.

WR Darius Cooper: The UDFA from Tarleton State is still just in his second NFL season. He made the roster again in 2026 and has 11 catches for 175 yards and 4 touchdowns this year. But he has caught just 45.8% of his targets, which is a very low number. Cooper hasn't been a star but he has made some big-time catches and should continue to get some run the rest of the way.

TE Eli Stowers: The second-round pick from Vanderbilt missed the first six games of the season on Injured Reserve with a quad injury. But Stowers made his NFL debut in Week 7 and then made his first NFL catches in Week 8. A couple of those catches came in garbage time but it was still good to see him finish with 3 for 53. When the Eagles drafted Stowers, we knew he was a developmental tight end. The best way to develop is to get him more playing time this season. Stowers is far from a finished product but he has the type of athleticism that's worth developing.

OL Willie Lampkin: The Eagles have had Landon Dickerson back in the starting lineup at left guard the last couple of weeks, but it'll be interesting to see if Dickerson remains with the team through the trade deadline. If not, maybe we see a bit more of Lampkin. The 5-foot-11 offensive lineman started a couple of games at left guard in between Cam Jurgens and Dickerson. Even if Lampkin can't get back on the field as a starter, the Eagles could find some unique ways to use him. He lined up as a fullback on the Saquon Barkley direct-snap touchdown on Sunday night.

OG Micah Morris: The sixth-round pick from Georgia has played just one snap all season so he probably won't get many chances to play this season. But if more injuries hit, Morris could get some chances. He's a really athletic rookie who is worth developing.

C Drew Kendall: The Eagles drafted Kendall out of Boston College in the fifth round last year and have been high on him for a while. We saw that this year when they got him into the starting lineup from Week 2. Kendall has now started five games at center this season and could be the Eagles' long-term fix at the position. The Eagles traded away his predecessor Cam Jurgens earlier this season, so if Kendall plays well the rest of the way, he could keep that job for a while.

OT Markel Bell: The third-round pick from Miami has had some ups and downs this season. Nick Sirianni seemed impressed by Bell's performance against the Cowboys, but Bell didn't have his best game against the Commanders. Lane Johnson started the first three games of the season before he abruptly retired and Bell has now started the last five games. According to PFF, Bell has given up 17 pressures and 5 sacks this season. He has had some good moments in the run game too. Johnson seemed really impressed by Bell in training camp; we'll see if Bell ends up being his long-term replacement.

DT TY Robinson: Robinson played 104 defensive snaps as a rookie fourth-round pick last year. Through eight games this season, Robinson has played just 52 snaps and is firmly behind Jalen Carter, Jordan Davis, Moro Ojomo and Byron Young on the depth chart. Robinson has played just 9% of the snaps, while Young has been on the field for 27%. Robinson had a solid training camp and preseason so it might make sense to start having him eat into Young's snaps.

LB Jihaad Campbell: This is a tricky one because Campbell began the season as a full-time starter next to Zack Baun and then the Eagles started up a rotation between Campbell and Jeremiah Trotter Jr. While it looks like Trotter might simply be the best option right now, Campbell was a first-round pick and has a ton of athletic ability. Campbell hasn't lived up to expectations but he's in Year 2 as a first-round pick. The only way for him to get better is to continue to play.

S Drew Mukuba: Mukuba is in a similar position as Campbell but there's no one like Trotter pushing him. The second-year, second-round pick from Texas has had a mostly disappointing season as a full-time starter in 2026. But he did have a good game against the Commanders with a forced fumble and an interception that was negated by a penalty. Through eight games, Mukuba has played 556 of 558 snaps. We might learn a lot about him over the final nine games of the season.

#2915. Duke Willing to Pay Opponent as Kara Lawson Faces Unexpected Scheduling Crisis Ahead of Sunday's Game

Duke was willing to pay another team just to play them. That is where Kara Lawson and the Blue Devils find themselves days before their next scheduled game. A program with Duke's resources is now subsidizing an opponent to keep a game on the calendar.

The Blue Devils were supposed to face North Texas on Sunday, November 8, in San Antonio. The matchup would have also marked the first-ever meeting between the two programs in women's basketball. However, the game has now been canceled. Tickets have also been reportedly canceled with Duke now scheduled to play FAU on Sunday.

"Duke was supposed to play North Texas in women's basketball on Sunday in San Antonio in "The Foundry Classic." That game is now canceled, according to multiple sources," shared USA Today's Mitchell Northam on X. "Duke is scrambling for a replacement opponent. North Texas will not reschedule a new opponent."

As a result, Lawson and the Blue Devils suddenly found themselves dealing with an unexpected scheduling headache. And in a bid to fill the unexpected gap in their schedule, Duke reportedly reached out to multiple conferences in search of a replacement opponent. The program was even willing to play on the road or pay another team to travel to Cameron Indoor Stadium in Durham, North Carolina, to face them.

Duke was scrambling to fill a gap in its schedule just days before Sunday's game. On Thursday night, Patriot League and Metro Conference head coaches received emails describing the Blue Devils' "urgent need" for a replacement opponent, three people familiar with the situation told USA TODAY Sports. Duke was willing to travel or pay a team to play as per the emails.

Originally, The Foundry Classic, which was announced as far back as September 22, was supposed to be a multi-day event. Apart from the basketball game, the event was also scheduled to feature a student-centered experience called The Foundry Future Summit, which would allow students to interact with college athletes off the court. There was also supposed to be a segment featuring Lawson and North Texas head coach Jason Burton in a moderated conversation called The Foundry Table.

Sadly, those plans have now fallen apart, and the Blue Devils have had to find another way to salvage their Sunday schedule. Fortunately for Lawson and her team, they appear to have successfully arranged a replacement matchup. Duke will now travel to Boca Raton, Florida, to face Florida Atlantic (FAU) on Sunday at 1 p.m. ET.

As for North Texas, a spokesperson for the program revealed that the Mean Green would not be searching for a last-minute replacement opponent. Instead, the team will use the unexpected break to regroup and prepare for its next outing. Following its Thursday night loss to San Diego State, North Texas will return to the court at home against Houston Christian on Wednesday, November 11.

So far, Duke women's basketball has played just one game this season, opening its 2026/27 campaign with a convincing 76-60 victory over Green Bay on November 3. Following the cancellation of its scheduled matchup against North Texas, the Blue Devils' second regular-season game will now come against Florida Atlantic on Sunday. Lawson's team will be looking to maintain its winning start and build some early momentum as it pursues its ambitions for the season.

The Blue Devils enter this campaign with plenty of expectations following back-to-back Atlantic Coast Conference Tournament titles. They have also reached the Elite Eight in each of the last two NCAA Tournament appearances, and the goal this season will be to take another step forward and put themselves in genuine contention for a national championship.

"You want to be in that situation," Lawson said. "You want to be sitting before a season or before a tournament as one of the favorites to win it. …It's where we want to be. We want to have high expectations. …The Final Four is not the line of demarcation for me. It's a national championship… Our goal is to put ourselves in position to be playing our best in March, and put ourselves in position to have a deep run. …We want to win."

With those ambitions in mind, the unexpected cancellation of the North Texas matchup is certainly not how Duke would have wanted to approach its second game of the season. However, with Florida Atlantic now providing a replacement opponent, Lawson and her players can turn their attention back to the court. The immediate task will be to secure another victory on Sunday as they begin their pursuit of a much bigger prize.

#2912. Eagles injury update: Joe Milton might not get much extra help

Eagles injury update: Joe Milton might not get much extra help originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles are switching quarterbacks for Week 9 but it doesn't look like Joe Milton is going to get a lot more help.

Several key starters were missing when the Eagles took the practice field on Wednesday afternoon as the team began to prepare for the Giants.

Here's the full injury report from Wednesday:

DNP – RB Saquon Barkley (hamstring/back), LB Jihaad Campbell (hamstring/quad), DB Michael Carter (abdomen), WR Britain Covey (knee), CB Jonathan Jones (hamstring), WR DeVonta Smith (hamstring), WR Dontayvion Wicks (ankle)

#2910. Real Madrid can re-sign former academy graduate for €10m amid Man United, Chelsea and Liverpool interest

Several Premier League clubs are monitoring Como defender Jacobo Ramon ahead of the summer 2027 transfer window, but Real Madrid have a significant advantage in the race thanks to a favourable buy-back clause.

According to AS, Manchester United are showing the strongest interest in the former Madrid academy graduate, while Chelsea and Liverpool are also keeping track of his development.

The 21-year-old has enjoyed an impressive campaign under Cesc Fabregas, establishing himself as an important figure in Como's defence.

He has made 14 appearances across all competitions, contributing three goals and two assists, earning him a place in Spain's senior national team squad for the first time.

Despite the growing interest, Ramon is not expected to leave Como during the January transfer window.

Rather, the attention surrounding his performances is instead building towards what could become an important transfer battle next summer.

Manchester United have a strong interest in Jacobo Ramon. (Photo by Valerio Pennicino/Getty Images)

The outlet mentions that Manchester United have been the most 'persistent club' in their attempts to monitor the Spanish defender.

Furthermore, the likes of Chelsea and Liverpool are also understood to have included him in their recruitment plans.

However, the situation remains at an early stage, and Ramon is currently focused on continuing his development in Italy, where regular football under Fabregas has helped him establish himself as one of Serie A's promising young centre-backs.

Real Madrid's previous agreement with Como is quite decisive here.

Notably, when Ramon moved to Italy for approximately €2.5 million, Madrid retained 50%of his economic rights and secured a buy-back option worth around €10 million, something that Real Madrid are reportedly planning for in 2027.

That arrangement gives the Spanish giants considerable control over his future.

Real Madrid have significant control over Jacobo Ramon's future. (Photo by Valerio Pennicino/Getty Images)

Even if Premier League clubs pursue a transfer, Real Madrid could bring him back for a relatively modest fee compared with his current reputation and performances.

For now, Real Madrid are taking a patient approach, with their defensive planning for 2027/28 influencing the decision.

As such, Antonio Rudiger's contract expires in 2027, while Eder Militao, Dean Huijsen, and Ibrahima Konate are expected to remain part of the defensive picture.

As reported before, Ramon is the leading candidate to return if Rudiger departs.

Consequently, Real Madrid currently have little reason to rush into a decision while his development at Como continues to progress.

#2908. How Tanner McKee found out he was benched and what he's doing about it

How Tanner McKee found out he was benched and what he's doing about it originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Nick Sirianni was asked point-blank on Monday if a quarterback change was on the table and he shot it down emphatically. The Eagles were sticking with Tanner McKee, Sirianni insisted.

"Tanner's our guy," Sirianni said on Monday afternoon. "We're not making any changes there.

The next day McKee got the call from Sirianni.

Despite what the head coach said to the world the day before, he was making a quarterback switch. McKee has been benched and Joe Milton, who arrived three weeks ago, will start in Week 9 against the Giants. The news broke on Wednesday.

"That's one thing I appreciate about (the team)," McKee said on Thursday. "They've been very upfront about things. I know what he said in the media on Monday, the day before. But he said he wanted to communicate to the guys before he says anything to the media. He doesn't want us finding out in the media, which I appreciate."

McKee, 26, said he isn't on social media, so he didn't know about Sirianni's declaration on Monday until after he found out he was demoted. So at least there's that.

Jalen Hurts began the year as the Eagles' starting quarterback but has been out since the Panthers game with blood clots that are claiming his season. McKee made the last three starts and the Eagles lost all of them. While McKee isn't the only reason they lost, he also wasn't able to do enough to win.

Over the last three weeks, McKee completed 63.2% of his passes for 638 yards with 2 touchdowns and 2 interceptions. He had a passer rating of 76.6 as the Eagles lost to the Panthers, Cowboys and Commanders. And there's no doubt that McKee struggled on Sunday in Washington, especially with his pocket awareness.

"I think, watching the film, different decisions, different throws," McKee said. "Obviously, as a quarterback, have to take accountability for things like that. I think you have to go back and review everything that was done even throughout the week. I thought I had a great process. Thought I felt prepared. I don't have any regrets there. I think you have to go back and be like, 'What could I have done there?' And take that approach as well."

The Eagles initially drafted McKee in the sixth round out of Stanford back in 2023 and groomed him to be their backup quarterback. That was the job he held all of last season coming off a Super Bowl parade. This offseason, though, the Eagles brought in veteran Andy Dalton to compete for that back job and Dalton won. But Dalton was demoted after throwing an interception against the Bears in Week 3 and was eventually released.

It's definitely fair to question how the Eagles have handled the quarterback position all year. McKee went from competing for the backup job to being the 3, then the 2, then the 1 and is now the 2 again.

And he's not resigned to staying in that spot.

"This is the NFL. Competition is always open," McKee said. "I'll keep our conversation private. I don't know how much [Sirianni has] been open to the media about our conversation. But there's always competition. This is the NFL. So you gotta go out, you've got to perform every week. I'm going to approach it as a competition and be ready in case my number is called. Whether that's an injury or my number is just called."

It's not like McKee got to play with a full deck over the last three weeks. He was without DeVonta Smith and Dontavyion Wicks for the last two of those losses and Saquon Barkley hasn't been healthy either. Not to mention, the offensive line hasn't played up to its standard all season.

But when McKee was asked on Thursday why he wasn't given the benefit of the doubt with all the injuries, he made no excuses.

"We have great players and we had great players on the field," McKee said. "The expectation is to go out and win and unfortunately, I fell short doing that. And I gotta be better and make better plays."

Because Milton arrived less than three weeks ago, McKee has been helping him as much as possible this week. Milton missed all of the install during training camp and has had to catch up quickly before his first NFL start on Sunday.

Even though Milton took his job, McKee has been more than willing to help him this week.

"In sports, you gotta have a short memory," McKee said. "I'm still a Philadelphia Eagle. I want the team to perform so anything I can do to help the team, whether that's helping Joe or Jalen or whoever it is, that's my job, that's my role and that's what I'm going to do."

As a 2023 draft pick, McKee is in the final year of his rookie contract with the Eagles. Based on the way this season has gone, there seems to be a pretty good chance he won't be back with the team in 2027.

But McKee isn't trying to rush that fate. McKee was asked on Thursday if he wants to still be here the rest of this season or if he'd prefer a clean break.

"I love it here," he said. "It's a great place. I have a lot of confidence in the coaches, in our scheme, Sean (Mannion). And so I'm happy to be here. I love being here. And I love to compete. Like we said, in the NFL, things are going to go up and down. It's going to be a roller coaster. But you have to love that as a competitor and you have to embrace that. That's my approach. That's what I'm going to do."

#2907. Nick Sirianni admits that he misled reporters about quarterback situation

How do you know when a football coach is lying? If he’s good at it, you don’t.

Because they all do. Particularly in so-called “must-lie” situations.

There’s an art to it. A way to conceal a lie with another lie. On Friday, Eagles coach Nick Sirianni opted not to lie about the fact that he lied on Monday when he told reporters that quarterback Tanner McKee would not be benched.

“Tanner’s our guy,” Sirianni said. “We’re not making any changes there.”

On Wednesday, the Eagles made a change there, elevating Joe Milton over McKee. The move was announced after Sirianni had his midweek media availability, which prevented him from being asked questions about the change in position from “Tanner’s our guy” to “Tanner’s not our guy.”

On Friday, Sirianni finally faced the music regarding his comments from Monday. In lieu of coming up with an explanation premised on the idea that what he said was true when he said it and circumstances changed, Sirianni surprisingly admitted that he wasn’t telling the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth to reporters and, in turn, the fans.

“I was going to hold onto that as long as I possibly could, every second, every minute,” Sirianni said. “Again, I’m not real shy with you guys. I’m going to tell you what I want to tell you guys. I respect the job that y’all have to do, but I’m going to tell you what I want to tell you guys because I’m always going to protect the team first and foremost.

“Every second that goes by in a situation like that, that you can hold onto information as long as you possibly can, is a little less time for the opponent to get ready for the game. I know there’s a lot of interest in everything that you all want to hear, the fans want to hear. My job is to win football games, do everything we can do to help win football games, protect the guys here on this football team, and handle everything that I can inside.”

In an industry where the truth is only told when it’s coincidental to strategic interests, it’s jarring that Sirianni told the truth that he was lying. It would have been easy to say, “We took a more careful look at the situation after Monday.” Or, even more simply, “We changed our mind. Shit happens.”

Instead, Sirianni opted to say, essentially, “Bullshit happens.” Instead of bothering to concoct a cover story regarding his lie, he said, essentially, “Yeah. I lied. I’ll do it whenever I feel like I have to.”

That’s the most important thing to remember. Coaches lie whenever they feel like they have to. Despite that fact, reporters and fans routinely take their words at face value, swallowing the bullshit while saying, “Thank you, sir, may I have another?”

So thank you, Nick, for making my job a little easier. In my first week of law school, one of the professors said, “The most important thing I can do for you is to give you a good crap filter.”

Because crap is everywhere. Usually, the people spewing it don’t admit it. When they do, it provides a tangible reminder as to whatever they say in the future.

For Sirianni, that’s the most problematic aspect of his decision to tell the truth about lying. There’s now an “Exhibit A” to which anyone and everyone can point as to anything and everything he says when assessing whether he’s being straight with reporters and, in turn, with fans.

#2904. In Roob's Eagles Stats: A big pile of misery broken up by tiny encouraging blips

In Roob's Eagles Stats: A big pile of misery broken up by tiny encouraging blips originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Thank God for Makai Lemon and Eli Stowers. Because without those two rookies, I wouldn't have much of a "Positive Section" in this week's Roob's Eagles Stats.

Even with them there aren't a whole lot of encouraging numbers surrounding this football team.

So with that in mind, here's this week's Roob's Eagles Stats. When you lose six in a row, the stats aren't pretty.

1. With Makai Lemon catching six passes for 57 yards and Eli Stowers three for 53 yards, this was only the second time in the last 36 years the Eagles have had two rookies over 50 receiving yards in the same game. The last time it happened was in 2021, when DeVonta Smith had 122 yards and Kenny Gainwell 58 in a loss to the Chiefs at the Linc. Before that in 1990 Fred Barnett had 55 yards and Calvin Williams 72 in a win over the Patriots at the Vet. Two rookie replacement players did it in a 1987 strike replacement game against the Cowboys, including Haverford High's Mike Siano, but last time it happened in a real road game was in 1973 with Charlie Young (112) and Don Zimmerman (51) in a loss to the 49ers at Candlestick Park.

2. Lemon now has 31 catches, 3rd-most among NFL rookies and only two fewer than rookie leader K.C. Concepcion of the Browns, who has 33. Germie Bernard of the Steelers has 32. Lemon is on pace for 65 catches, which would be 3rd-most in Eagles history by a rookie, behind Keith Jackson (81 in 1988) and Jordan Matthews (67 in 2014). With seven catches against Dallas and six against Washington, Lemon tied the franchise record of consecutive games by a rookie with six or more receptions. Seven others have done it, most recently Jeremy Maclin in 2009 and DeVonta Smith in 2021.

3. With his fourth sack on Sunday night, Jalen Carter now has 11 ½ in his career and moved into a tie for 7th-most career sacks by an Eagles interior lineman. He's just 1 ½ behind Javon Hargrave for sixth. The top five are Andy Harmon and Fletcher Cox (20 ½), Jerome Brown (19), Darwin Walker (17) and Kenny Clarke (16 ½). With his fourth sack Nolan Smith surpassed his total of 3.0 from all of last year and is now just 2 ½ sacks shy of his career high of 6 ½ in 2024, not including four more from the postseason.

OK, thus begins the reality section. Brace yourself. This is bad. Really bad.

4. The Eagles have had fewer than 350 yards of offense in all eight games so far this year, with a high of 341 at Tennessee. This is the first time in more than half a century they've opened a season with eight games under 350 net yards. Last time it happened was the first 11 games of the 1971 season, when their yardage high was 315 yards in a 37-14 loss to the Bengals in the season opener at Riverfront Stadium. Going back to last year, the Eagles have gone 10 straight games with fewer than 350 yards, their longest streak since an 11-gamer over the last nine games of 1977 and the first games of 1978. This is not good.

5. The Eagles have been outscored by 65 points through eight games, the most points they've been outscored by in their first eight games since 1998, when they were outscored by 117 points. You have to go back to 1972 to find the last time before 1998 they were outscored by this many points eight games into a season. The only NFL team outscored by a larger margin this year is the 0-8 Chargers, who have been outscored by 76 points. The Eagles have been outscored by 11 points by the 3-4 Panthers and by 17 points by the 2-4-1 Commanders and 3-5 Cowboys.

6. This is the first time since 1970 the Eagles have lost three games in a season by 11 or more points to teams with losing records. It's only the third season in franchise history they've lost two games by 17 or more points to teams with losing records. It also happened in 1972 and 2015. The only other time they lost back-to-back games by at least 17 points to losing teams came in 2015, when they lost 45-17 to the Bucs, who finished 6-10, and 45-14 to a Lions team that finished 7-9. Chip Kelly was fired four weeks later.

7. The Eagles led 10-0 Sunday before losing 27-10. Last time the Eagles led a game by 10 points in the first quarter and lost by at least 17 points was in 2011, when they took a 10-0 lead on the Patriots at the Linc on a LeSean McCoy touchdown and Alex Henery field goal before getting outscored 38-10 the rest of the game and losing 38-20, Tom Brady over Vince Young. This was the first game in franchise history where the Eagles led by 10 points in the first quarter and didn't score again.

8. With a 25-14 loss to the Panthers, 38-21 to the Cowboys and 27-10 to the Commanders, this is only the third time in the last 50 years the Eagles have lost three straight games by 11 or more points, allowing at least 25 points in all three. It also happened in 2012 and 2016. Last time the Eagles were outscored by 71 points in a six-game span was 2012.

9. Rasul Douglas's interception near the end of the game was the 23rd of his 10-year career with the Eagles, Panthers, Packers, Cards, Bills, Dolphins and Commanders. Douglas is one of only three Eagles drafted in the last 30 years with at least 23 career interceptions. The others are Sheldon Brown (26) and Jordan Poyer (25). Douglas, the Eagles' 3rd-round pick in 2017, is the first cornerback drafted by the Eagles to record an interception against the Eagles since Mark McMillian of the Chiefs picked off Rodney Peete in a 24-21 Chiefs win at the Vet in 1998.

10. The Eagles netted just 268 yards Sunday night, their fifth game during the six-game losing streak with fewer than 270 yards. They're averaging 244 yards per game during these last six games, their worst six-game span in 27 years, since they averaged 241 during a six-game span in 1999, Andy Reid's first season as head coach. This is the first time they've had five games under 270 yards in a six-game span since early in the 2003 season. No other NFL team has had five games under 270 yards this year. Their overall average of 265 yards per game is their worst through eight games since 1999, when they were averaging 254 through their eighth game.

#2901. Grant Nelson leads Long Island with double-double but Nets fall to Greensboro

The Long Island Nets had several individual performances worth recognizing in Friday's G League opener and after all development is the point, but they ultimately couldn't do enough to overcome the Greensboro Swarm, falling 121-111 at Novant Health Fieldhouse.

The game was close throughout with 12 lead changes and three ties. After going down 60-55 at the half, Long Island was still down only four, 87-83 at the end of the third, the Swarm took over, outscoring the Nets, 34-28.

It was a frustrating result for Long Island, considering the contributions it received from multiple players, whether on assignment from Brooklyn, two-ways or Exhibit 10s. Arguably, the biggest positive came from two-way Grant Nelson, who delivered an impressive double-double and established himself as the team's leading performer … at least for opening night.

The 24-year-old 7-footer finished with 18 points and 13 rebounds in 27 minutes. The big man was active throughout his time on the floor, shooting 8-of-16 from the field and collecting six offensive rebounds. His ability to create additional possessions off the glass gave the Nets an important presence inside. The only negative for the Alabama product was his failure to hit on any of his five 3-pointers.

Nelson added two assists, one steal, and one block for a solid all-around performance. In a game that ended in a 10-point defeat, Nelson's performance was the clearest bright spot for the Nets.

Nelson's performance is an especially encouraging first step considering how limited his opportunities were last season. He appeared in just four games with Brooklyn, spending much of his time trying to establish himself despite knee issues that he says are now resolved after a summer of rehab.

There were also positive performances from two of the Nets 2026 picks.

Joshua Jefferson, taken with the 28th pick, had a solid showing with 15 points, six rebounds, three assists and only one turnover. The Iowa State product shot 5-of-9 from the field and connected on three of his seven attempts from beyond the arc. A 6'9", 240-pound point forward, he was selected by Brooklyn primarily because of his versatility which includes an ability to score from the perimeter while helping on the glass.

Tyler Bilodeau, the 6'9" sharpshooter taken at No. 43 in June, also provided a valuable offensive contribution, finishing with 13 points, four rebounds, and two assists. He also recorded five steals, giving Long Island an impact beyond his scoring. Bilodeau's five steals marked the highest number of thefts ever by a Long Island Nets player in a NBA G League debut.

The 2026 second-round selection out of UCLA, who like Nelson is on a two-way contract, shot 5-of-10 from the field and 3-of-7 from 3-point range.

On the other hand, Drake Powell, one of two members of the Flatbush 5 assigned to Long Island, had a difficult night offensively. The second year player finished with six points on 2-of-6 shooting, missing all three of his attempts from beyond the arc. He grabbed four rebounds and recorded three assists. He did record a block and a steal but the Nets needed more production from him. It was a game where his shot simply wasn't falling.

Ben Saraf, the other Brooklyn Nets player on assignment, also struggled to find his shooting touch. The 6'6" 20-year-old finished with eight points on 3-of-14 shooting from the field and just 1-of-8 from 3-point range. He had opportunities to make an impact as a scorer but couldn't get enough shots to fall. He also finished with three turnovers in 32 minutes.

There was at least one positive to take away from Saraf's performance: his playmaking. The young guard recorded a team-high seven assists, showing his ability to create opportunities. Defensively, he also added two steals. Saraf, a 2025 first-round pick who came to Brooklyn from German club Ratiopharm Ulm, is still adjusting to the NBA system and the professional game in North America.

Beyond the draft picks and two ways, there were some encouraging signs among the guys hoping to move up the G League ladder.

Isaiah Wong is the most experienced Long Island player, having played more than 20 games in the NBA as well as a season in the Spanish league. The 6'4" guard finished with 16 points, six steals, two assists and one rebound in 31 minutes. He shot an efficient 7-of-12 from the field and 2-of-4 from three-point range.

Dion Brown was another bright spot off the bench, recording 15 points in just 16 minutes. The St. Louis product shot 6-of-9 from the field and 3-of-5 from beyond the arc. Brown went undrafted in the 2026 NBA Draft but played well in the Summer League.

One of the most encouraging performances came from Dain Dainja, who made an immediate impression in his first game with the team. Coming off the bench, the 6'9" big with the gargantuan 7'7" wingspan finished with 16 points, six rebounds and three blocks in 21 minutes. He was efficient offensively, shooting 6-of-10 from the field.

Long Island won't have to wait long for an opportunity to respond . The Nets will remain in Greensboro for a rematch against the Swarm on Saturday, November 7, continuing the two-game opening-weekend series at Novant Health Fieldhouse.

After allowing 121 points in the opener, Long Island will look to make defensive adjustments and find a way to slow down Greensboro while building on the positives from Nelson's double-double and the contributions from its bench.

Boxscore: Greensboro Swarm 121, Long Island Nets 111 – G League

#2897. Eagles-Giants matchups to watch in Week 9

Eagles-Giants matchups to watch in Week 9 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-6) host the Giants (4-2-1) on Sunday afternoon at Lincoln Financial Field.

While the Eagles are on a six-game losing streak, the Giants are in first place in the NFC East and coming off a bye week.

The Eagles have been having a tough time with blitzes all year and they're probably going to see a lot of them this week against the Giants and defensive coordinator Dennard Wilson. Maybe Joe Milton will be able to avoid them better than Tanner McKee.

Milton, 26, will make his first-career start on Sunday. While he isn't exactly a dual-threat QB, Milton has more ability to avoid pressure.

This season, the Eagles have faced 109 blitzes this season and have given up 66 pressures — a pressure rate of 60.6%, which is the highest in the NFL. They have also allowed a free rusher more than any other team in the league. So it would be crazy for teams to not blitz them at this point. And the Giants already blitz at the second highest rate in the NFL, per NextGen Stats. And the Vikings barely count because Brian Flores is playing a different sport right now:

1. Vikings: 71.0%2. Giants: 40.3%3. Buccaneers: 40.2%4. Patriots: 40.1%5. Panthers: 39.6%

Wilson was with the Eagles in 2021 and 2022 and was passed over for the defensive coordinator job in 2023, when the Eagles went with Sean Desai. He has since served as DC with the Titans and now Giants.

We've watched Saquon Barkley (hamstring/back) try to gut it out for the last two games and with the bye week coming up, he'll probably try to do it again against his former team on Sunday afternoon. Barkley managed to play 22 snaps against the Commanders in Week 8 and wasn't very productive. He had 13 carries for just 30 yards and a touchdown, as well as a 24-yard catch. But Barkley is still obviously the Eagles' best option. Even playing through these injuries, Barkley has shown how good he still is. And the Eagles have already told him they're not moving him ahead of the trade deadline.

The Giants this season have the No. 26-ranked run defense, giving up 128.1 yards per game. Before their bye week, the Giants held the Texans to just 84 rushing yards in an overtime game but gave up 175 to the Saints and 202 to the Commanders. So they might still be susceptible on the ground. The Giants are giving up 1.66 yards before contact per attempt, which ranks 27th in the league. The Eagles will have the same OL again this week for the third straight game.

The Giants have some very good edge rushers but the team hasn't gotten many sacks this season. They are tied with the Chiefs and Dolphins at the bottom of the league with 9.0 in seven games. (The Eagles have just 10 in eight games.)

But the Giants still have Abdul Carter, Kayvon Thibodeaux and rookie Arvell Reese to bring the pressure. And then they have a DC who is always willing to add more pressure by blitzing. This won't be an easy game for Jordan Mailata and Markel Bell. Bell, the rookie from Miami, had a rough game against the Commanders in Week 8 and has had some ups and downs since taking over for Lane Johnson. But the Eagles need to really figure out what they have in him over the final nine games of the season. This will be a good test on Sunday.

The Giants are coming off the bye week but it's unclear if that time off was enough to get Cam Skattebo back from a hamstring injury. The Giants' starting running back hurt his hammy in a run late in the Giants' overtime win over the Texans before the bye. If Skattebo is unavailable, the Giants would have Devin Singletary and Gregory Desroisiers as their top backs.

Skattebo this season leads the Giants in rushing with 418 yards on 98 carries (4.3) and 2 touchdowns. Skattebo is a battering ram, who is averaging 3.6 yards after contact per rush. The Eagles have had some tackling issues this season, so they'll need to get him to the ground.

Much has been made about the Eagles' inability to rush the passer this year, and that's true. But it starts because they're unable to stop the run. Jordan Davis is supposed to be their run-stuffing anchor but he has been dealing with a hand injury that has slowed him down. The Eagles are 28th in run defense this season, allowing 131.9 yards per game. The Eagles have given up 100+ rushing yards in every single game this season. That's their longest such streak to start a season since 1968.

The Giants used the No. 6 overall pick to draft Nabers out of LSU in the 2024 season and he's on pace to have a career year in 2026. As a rookie, Nabers went for over 1,200 yards but tore his ACL in Year 2 and missed most of the season. Nabers has come back from that ACL tear better than ever this season. Through seven games, he has 48 catches on 70 targets for 605 yards and 2 touchdowns. He's averaging 86.4 yards per game and has had over 100 yards in each of his last four games. Nabers lines up a bit in the slot but is mostly outside. The Eagles haven't used Quinyon Mitchell to travel with top receivers yet this season but it might be a good week to try it.

Mitchell has been excellent this season but proved he's human when he gave up a 53-yard pass to Treylon Burks in the third quarter. But Mitchell has been excellent all season. He has given up 27 catches on 50 targets for 293 yards. So 18% of the yards he has given up this season have come on one play. He has an opponent passer rating of 69.8 this year.

Through eight games this season, Jalen Carter already has four sacks and is on pace to have a career-high in 2026. But that obviously doesn't tell the whole story. At times, Carter looks dominant, but he hasn't been consistent enough. Even Vic Fangio has admitted that this season. But if the Eagles' defense is going to figure things out in 2026, then Carter has to be the motor behind it.

In this game, Carter will get some matchups against Giants first-round pick Francis Mauigoa. The Giants used the No. 10 overall pick on the offensive lineman out of Miami. But because they didn't have a spot at offensive tackle, the Giants have moved him inside the right guard for his rookie season. While Mauigoa has had some ups and downs this season, he has flashed some real potential.

#2896. Brandon Aiyuk plans to meet with 49ers at team's business offices, not team facility

49ers tight end George Kittle was right, sort of. Receiver Brandon Aiyuk won’t “get in” the team facility on Friday, because he’s not trying to.

Per agent Drew Rosenhaus, Friday’s meeting will happen at the team’s business offices and not at the team’s practice facility.

That makes the session not a formal reporting-for-duty by Aiyuk, in the hopes of forcing the team’s hand. As explained earlier today, the NFL still hasn’t reinstated Aiyuk from the reserve/left squad list — even though Rosenhaus filed the request on October 27.

Instead, it’s a meeting presumably aimed at negotiating a resolution to the situation.

The 49ers pushed to reporters on Thursday night the idea that Aiyuk’s return to the NFL is tied to the $13 million the team believes he owes in previously paid signing bonus. That shouldn’t be part of the analysis as to whether he’s reinstated by the league, whether the 49ers put him on the roster again, and/or whether the 49ers cut him.

But they’re making it an issue, because they can tie his ability to play to his willingness to wire the disputed funds to the team.

So that’s what’s happening. The brakes already have been applied to what should have been an administrative and automatic process. The league and the 49ers are working together to help the team get money back from Aiyuk before Aiyuk is permitted to get back into the league.

Then there’s the fact that an active arrest warrant is pending for Aiyuk, arising from a speeding incident. The business meeting could potentially be interrupted by an attempt to enforce a specific item of police business.

#2894. San Antonio vs. Sacramento, Final Score: Spurs’ talent outweighs sloppy moments for 7th straight win, 124-109

The San Antonio Spurs got their first taste of Emirates Cup play on their successful trip to the home of the struggling Sacramento Kings. Despite the Kings being overmatched in talent, they gave a solid effort looking for their second win that made the Spurs sweat at times.

Things started getting ugly as the visitors kept plugging away on the glass and raised their lead to 16 points in the non-Victor Wembanyama minutes thanks in part to their 3-point shooting, ball security and Keldon Johnson's hustle. They went on to have their second-highest scoring first half of the season (76), but had trouble getting back into their flow in the third quarter.

Their large cushion was soon sliced nearly in half until Wemby led the charge that got them back on track. There were some defensive lapses in the following period, but the Spurs quickly atoned for them with a 3-pointer or a move inside.

They were playing like they thought it would be easy early, allowing open 3-point attempts and transition baskets, but the flow of the game started changing when the Spurs started consistently getting to the line. The trickle effect was forcing the Kings to slow down offensively and take more bad shots, which put the Spurs in the transition.

Wembanyama was merciless, scoring 30 points on 55.6 shooting at short and long range, while adding 14 rebounds and two blocks. He took his final rest with eight minutes left as the Spurs were up 22 points.

Darius Acuff Jr. has been the league's highest-scoring rookie, yet he can be a bit of a chucker, so the Spurs defended him mostly without help and in drop coverage on screen rolls. The strategy was more successful in the first half, holding him to 37.5 percent shooting, yet he started getting loose after.

For a while, the Spurs were making mincemeat of the Kings, even having Acuff and Leaky Black frustrated with each other within five minutes of the game. It started with Devin Vassell's early marksmanship helping steady the offense, and then Victor Wembanyama's inside pressure piled on, leaving them without answers. The Kings would also go to a zone when Wemby sat, but the ball movement made them pay for it.

The Kings do a bad job of helping the helper, which resulted in tons of clean outside looks and the Spurs setting a new season high in 3-point percentage. Wemby and Vassell combined for nine 3-point makes.

The Spurs had a rough start to the third quarter, making 25 percent of their attempts through eight minutes. The Kings made four 3-pointers in that span, yet the flow started slowly turning back to the Spurs because the Kings could not defend without fouling, again. To boot, the Spurs went on to shot the most free throws of the season (42).

In the first half, Stephon Castle was too strong on the move to be denied. He wasn't as assertive later but finished an emphatic lob supplied by Wemby. He finished with 19 points on 53.8 percent shooting with seven assists against one turnover.

Crisis averted: Domantas Sabonis committed a flagrant one by sticking his foot under Wembanyama's landing space on a 3-point attempt, causing his ankle to roll. Wemby made two-of-three at the line and subsequently looked fine in the next half.

De'Aaron Fox didn't have the biggest night on the stat sheet, but made good reads that helped take and maintain the lead.

Aside from the trophy, each player on the winning side of the in-season tournament gets $604,985.

#2889. Diving deeper into Cavs blowout win over 76ers: Cleveland plays their most complete game of season

CLEVELAND — The Cleveland Cavaliers avenged their opening-night collapse by thoroughly outplaying the Philadelphia 76ers for 48 minutes, leading to a 119-95 victory.

The Cavs need Mitchell to look to get teammates involved, but that can't come at the expense of his aggressiveness. The start to this game was the prime example of the balance you want to see from Mitchell.

Mitchell got the scoring going with two layups attacking the basket and then a running jumper in transition in the first minute and a half of the game. That opened up avenues for him to find his teammates, which he did during the rest of the first quarter, resulting in three assists.

That opening set the tempo for what was a complete game from Mitchell. He was the undisputed best player on the court. It's no surprise the Cavs won the minutes he played by 28.

"He feels the moment, he knows we needed this win," head coach Kenny Atkinson said. "He made some great passes too. He was very aggressive tonight."

22 points, 6 rebounds, 4 assists, 1 steal, 1 block

Mobley filled in admirably for Jarrett Allen at center. He controlled the paint on both ends and showed his range as a shooter, going 3-for-4 from deep. Comfortably hitting threes like he has been this season opens up Cleveland's entire offense and allows him to easily fit at the four and the five. This was the ideal version of Mobley.

"He's really playing great basketball," Atkinson said.

15 points, 10 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 steal

The Cavs needed rebounding and a scoring punch from the bench considering who was out of the lineup. Hezonja provided both. He took advantage of mismatches inside, going 4-for-4 on shots inside the arc.

Hezonja is slowly becoming an integral part of this team. His presence has become invaluable over the first nine games.

8 points on 3-of-12 shooting, 7 rebounds, 6 turnovers

You could make a convincing argument that the Cavs would be at the top of the conference if James chose them in free agency instead. Cleveland has dropped games due to a failure to close out contests and poor rebounding. James would help immensely in both aspects. That said, those benefits didn't exactly come through in this dreadful performance.

James wasn't able to establish any kind of rhythm. He couldn't create any separation whenever he attacked off the dribble, and he wasn't able to get out into the open court. On top of that, he had only two more points than he had giveaways. That all added up to arguably his worst professional game ever played in Cleveland.

Embiid struggled mightily. He had a difficult time getting around Mobley and generating clean looks. This resulted in going 0-for-9 from the field. On the other end, he couldn't move laterally quickly enough to hang with Mobley.

This wasn't a standout offensive performance, but his energy and effort on the glass made him a difference maker. That matters for a player who is being asked to fill very different roles depending on who he's sharing the court with.

Meleek Thomas plays with a confidence that can't be taught. Sequences like this show that.

Cavs fans gave James a respectful, but apathetic welcome home.

The Cavs took 43% of their shots at the rim (92nd percentile). This allowed them to outscore the Sixers 50-30 in the paint.

This game, however, was different, due to the contributions of their three stars.

Mitchell, James Harden, and Mobley all fulfilled their roles perfectly. Mitchell was dynamic on ball as a scorer and passer. That energy carried the offense. Harden battled defensively and kept the offense on schedule. His steady hand helped Cleveland maintain their grasp of the game. Mobley controlled the paint on both ends, taking advantage of his slower frontcourt opponents.

There's a downstream effect to the stars playing well. The rest of the rotation was able to more easily slot into their roles, even though they were shorthanded. Players like Meleek Thomas, Tyrese Proctor, and Mario Hezonja have much simpler and more defined roles when they're able to play off the stars and aren't forced to pick up the slack. That makes it easier for them to excel, as they did.

This all added up to a convincing and thorough beatdown of the team with the most wins in the league while also being the antithesis of the opening-night collapse.

The Cavs have shown incredible highs and low lows through nine games. At this point, we don't know who this team will be on a night-to-night basis, and they haven't quite discovered their identity. Yet, the talent is there. If they can put the pieces together, they have the potential to be dangerous.

#2888. Manchester United challenging Juventus for Serie A star

Marco Carnesecchi has emerged as one of the most highly regarded Italian goalkeepers, attracting interest from several major European clubs. Juventus have followed his progress for years and remain keen to bring him to Turin as they look to find a permanent solution in goal.

The Bianconeri have monitored Carnesecchi since his academy days at Atalanta and continued tracking him during his loan spell at Cremonese. That experience helped him develop into an accomplished goalkeeper, and he has since established himself as Atalanta's first choice.

Juventus view him as a quality option for the future and have continued to follow his situation closely in recent weeks. The position is one they intend to address ahead of next season, with the club keen to secure a reliable long-term solution.

The Turin side have already made several changes to their goalkeeping department. They signed two goalkeepers on loan during the last transfer window and also brought in Neto on a short-term deal as a free agent after Kamil Grabara suffered a season-ending injury.

Despite those moves, Carnesecchi remains firmly on their radar. His performances for Atalanta have kept him among the players Juventus would like to sign, but they may now have to compete with another major club.

According to Tuttomercatoweb, Manchester United are also interested in securing the Italian's signature and could even attempt to sign him during the January transfer window.

(Photo by Francesco Scaccianoce/Getty Images)

The Premier League club reportedly regard Carnesecchi as one of the best goalkeepers in world football and is determined to strengthen their options in that position. Their willingness to consider an early move could complicate Juventus' plans.

United's interest means the Bianconeri may face serious competition if they decide to pursue the Atalanta goalkeeper. A January approach would also give the English side an opportunity to move ahead of their rivals before the summer transfer window.

Juventus have admired Carnesecchi for a long time, but that interest alone may not be enough to secure his services. With Manchester United also keen, the race for his signature could become an important transfer battle in the coming months.

#2885. Is Luka Dončić already an MVP lock? Lakers star continues torrid start with 53-point outburst

LOS ANGELES – Walker Kessler had as good a look as anybody inside Crypto.com Arena at the biggest shot of the night.

The Los Angeles Lakers big man grabbed a Sidy Cissoko airball with six seconds left in the first half, handed the ball to Luka Dončić, set a screen for him and let the Luka Magic happen from there. Dončić launched a 62-footer from the top of the opposite key. The crowd held their breath with what felt more like anticipation than anxiety as the ball floated high in the air before coming back down and banking into the net for yet another unreal buzzer-beater.

"He's incredible to watch," Walker said after the Lakers' 124-118 win over the Portland Trail Blazers on Friday. "He's incredible to play alongside. He's my MVP. I think it's pretty clear right now."

Trail Blazers head coach Micah Nori had the utmost praise for Dončić when he spoke with reporters at Crypto.com Arena on Friday afternoon, saying the Slovenian superstar "can do anything in the world."

Dončić proved his opponent's point in the closing seconds of the first half.

What made the shot even more notable, perhaps, was that the Lakers had collectively struggled shooting the ball up to that point, starting 0-for-15 from 3-point range and falling behind by as many as 16. Dončić's shot at the end of the half was just LA's fourth make from deep in 23 attempts; it also sent the Lakers into the locker room having cut the Portland lead to just seven points.

"I think it changes the whole energy, in the team and in the gym. I thought it was an important shot to make," Dončić said before cracking a smile. "So that's why I decided to make it."

But it's not just Dončić who has an unshakeable confidence when it comes to that kind of shot, which he works on meticulously after practices and shootarounds. His teammates are starting to expect them to go in.

"At this point, it's pretty normal for him," Jake LaRavia said. "It's high difficulty for a lot of players, but it's pretty easy for him. I feel like he's just been playing at such a high level. Incredible offensive talent."

And true to what Dončić expected, a switch flipped for the rest of the night.

The Lakers dominated the third quarter, outscoring the Trail Blazers 34-15 in the frame behind 20 points from Dončić, who has now dropped at least 40 in four consecutive games, joining Kobe Bryant, Elgin Baylor and George Mikan as the only players in franchise history to do so. Dončić finished the night with a season-high 53 points, five rebounds and five assists. That brings his average up to an unthinkable 40.1 points per game, something that hasn't been accomplished since Wilt Chamberlain.

"I'm having a lot of fun," Dončić said. "Obviously playing basketball, it's a lot of fun for me. It's more when you play like this and when you win games. It's been like this my whole life. Basketball was my dream. Playing in the NBA was my dream."

The 27-year-old has completely kicked in the door and is dominating the early MVP conversation amid this historic hot streak.

Dončić currently leads Lorenzo Reyes' NBA MVP rankings, and a performance like the one on Friday only furthers the hold Dončić has on the top spot. Just listen to the multiple "M-V-P" chants that echoed through the building at various points in the night.

Not that MVP discussions have much meaning at all 10 games into an 82-game season. Head coach JJ Redick himself said pregame he doesn't get into those talks until they're at least 50 games in. But the level Dončić has been playing at makes it hard not to at least acknowledge it.

Redick, who has previously called Dončić a supernova, was asked if he's running out of superlatives to describe his best player. His response?

"Sure. I'm gonna just sit around and we'll gather one day, come up with more superlatives so I have more words ready," he said sarcastically. "For now, I don't have any words."

The Lakers look like the real deal early on. Their chemistry is off the charts; that much was on full display with their 17 total assists on Friday, or in Kessler's 16-point, 12-rebound night, or Austin Reaves' 25 points.

But Dončić has been the engine behind it all. He's playing like the best player in the NBA, and the team is that much better because of it.

#2882. Future Eagles Fits: Four position versatile OTs to know in the 2027 NFL Draft

Future Eagles Fits: Four position versatile OTs to know in the 2027 NFL Draft originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Welcome back to Future Eagles Fits, our weekly deep dive into the prospects who could eventually find their way to Philadelphia.

Every week, we're going inside the tape and looking at prospects through an Eagles-centric lens — breaking down the athletic profile, positional versatility and scheme fit that could ultimately determine whether a player makes sense in Philly. Along the way, we'll examine how prospects are being evaluated across the league as draft boards begin to take shape and conversations are had with representatives across the scouting landscape.

If there's one position group that could be a priority for the Eagles heading into next spring, it's the offensive line. The Eagles have always placed an emphasis on building through the trenches, but the group has undergone significant changes over the last year. Cam Jurgens is a Baltimore Raven, Lane Johnson announced his retirement and rookie Markell Bell has been thrust into an opportunity at right tackle. Meanwhile, Jordan Mailata, one of the best left tackles in football, has regressed, Drew Kendall has had his share of ups and downs in a new role at the pivot spot and Tyler Steen hasn't been close to the level of play he displayed last fall.

There are plenty of questions surrounding the group, and whether the issues stem from coaching, the overall offensive scheme, individual performance or some combination of all three, the Eagles have work to do up front. Howie Roseman has never been shy about investing in the position group, and with multiple potential starting positions to address, adding more young talent could be a significant priority next spring.

The bottom line when it comes to offensive linemen, a position group that has consistently been a foundation of Philly's success, is that you can never have enough quality bodies up front. Whether it's finding a versatile interior blocker who can compete at multiple positions or adding another long-term option at tackle, the Eagles should be looking at opportunities to add talent throughout the draft.

Here are four positional versatile offensive tackles to know for the 2027 NFL Draft — one potential Day 1 target, two Day 2 prospects and a Day 3 option on the rise for teams.

Day 1: Lance Heard, Kentucky | 6'5″, 323 lbs, 36 ¼ arm (verified measurements)

Heard is one of the more physically imposing offensive line prospects in this class, and he's currently a top five player on my personal board.

A Tennessee transfer, Heard brings an impressive combination of size, length, raw power and violence that immediately stands out when watching his tape similar to guys like Tyler Smith out of Tulsa and Paris Johnson Jr. out of Ohio State a few years back. He looks to staple players to the turf on every single rep, and at 320-plus pounds with ridiculous 36-inch arms, he has the type of physical profile NFL teams drool over along the offensive line. Describing him as powerful would be an understatement, as he can flat out overwhelm defenders at the point of attack, generate movement in the run game and use his length to create separation between himself and opposing rushers with ease.

What makes Heard such an intriguing player is that there's still plenty of room for development. He has the natural strength and physical tools, but his consistency, technique and ability to maintain proper positioning throughout reps will be important areas of development. There are moments when his physical advantages take over, and others where he can become a little too aggressive with his approach. Working on becoming more consistent with his hands, balance and overall technique will be important as he makes the transition to the next level.

An interesting conversation surrounding Heard is where he ultimately projects at the NFL level. While he has experience playing tackle and possesses the length to remain outside, there are teams who believe his best position as a pro could actually be inside at guard. His power and ability to create movement inside make him an intriguing option along the interior, particularly for a team like the Eagles who value athleticism and physicality in the run game.

For the Eagles, that positional flexibility is particularly appealing. With questions surrounding Landon Dickerson's long-term future at left guard and the possibility of additional movement along the offensive line, Heard could provide an opportunity to address multiple needs with one selection. He has the physical profile to compete at tackle, but the possibility of moving inside could give the Eagles another option as it continues to reshape the group.

Day 2: Trevor Lauck, Iowa | 6'5″, 314 lbs, 33 ¼ arm (verified measurements)

Iowa continues to produce NFL-caliber offensive linemen, and Lauck is the next name to know from Kirk Ferentz's program.

A technically sound athlete up front with experience at left tackle, Lauck offers a different profile than some of the more physically imposing offensive tackles in this class. He's not necessarily going to overwhelm defenders with overwhelming length or elite raw power, but his ability to stay balanced, maintain proper positioning and execute his assignments makes him an appealing prospect. Linemen at Iowa come out extremely well coached, something that can't be said for a majority of prospects at the collegiate level no matter how talented a prospect they are.

On tape, Lauck's technique and overall consistency are what stand out. He's comfortable operating in space, displays good footwork in pass pro and does a nice job staying connected to defenders through the duration of a rep. His hand placement and ability to maintain leverage allow him to neutralize rushers without needing to rely exclusively on his physical tools, and in the run game, he's a coordinated blocker who understands angles to showcase the ability to work up to the second level and sustain blocks.

For the Eagles, Lauck is a name to keep an eye on because of the potential to add another technically sound young blocker to the unit. Philly has always valued players who can operate within a physical run scheme while providing consistency in pass protection, and Lauck checks and bolds several of those boxes. Whether he ultimately sticks at tackle or projects better inside, his ability to execute assignments and provide positional flexibility could make him an appealing Day 2 target.

Day 2: Trevor Goosby, Texas | 6'7″, 325 lbs, 34 ⅞ arm (verified measurements)

Goosby entered the 2026 season with legitimate first-round buzz and was widely viewed as one of the top offensive tackle prospects in the country. However, a torn patellar tendon in his left knee ended his campaign abruptly.

At 6-foot-7 with nearly 35-inch arms, Goosby has the type of frame and athletic profile that immediately draws the eyes of NFL scouts. And that's before you turn on the tape. He's a long, athletic tackle with the ability to cover ground in pass protection, mirror opposing edge rushers and use his length and outstanding patience to keep defenders away from the quarterback. His movement skills are particularly impressive for someone with his size, and he has shown the ability to recover when defenders attempt to counter his initial pass set.

Goosby's development as a pass protector has been encouraging, particularly after earning first-team All-SEC honors in 2025. He has the athleticism to handle speed off the edge, and his length gives him a significant advantage when he's able to establish his hands early in a rep. He's got vines for arms, and when he's able to 'win first' with his hands he can take the air out of a pass rush quickly.

The biggest question surrounding Goosby now is his health and what his next step looks like. A severe knee injury introduces an entirely different element into his draft evaluation, particularly with the possibility of returning to Texas for another season still on the table. Prior to the injury, he was viewed as a potential top-10 guy. If he elects to enter the draft, his medical evaluations and recovery timeline will play a significant role in determining where he ultimately comes off the board.

With Johnson's retirement and Bell still developing, the Eagles have a need for additional talent at the position. The injury creates some risk, but the upside is significant if he's able to make a full recovery. Depending on how the medical evaluations unfold and whether he declares, Goosby could become one of the more interesting value opportunities in the class.

Day 3: Samson Okunlola, Miami | 6'6″, 324 lbs, 33 ¾ arm (verified measurements)

Evaluating Okunlola is an exercise in patience, but his raw traits are undeniable. A former five-star recruit, he took time in Coral Gables before breaking into the rotation in 2025, serving as a rotational force at left guard throughout Miami's 16-game CFP run. The film he put together during that stretch was extremely promising as he allowed just five total pressures all year, per PFF, posting shutouts against Ohio State and Indiana (National Championship) when it mattered most. His foundation is built on elite athleticism, flexibility in his lower half and a massive frame that naturally creates movement in the run game by establishing angles and washing defenders down the line of scrimmage. His ability to sink and drive in his lower half is unique for a player of his stature, as larger athletes usually bend at the hips, not the knees, which can cause them to lose reps consistently against more agile defenders. Not Okunlola.

However, the developmental hurdles are real and also why context is important when discussing players and scheme fit. It's not a linear path from college dominance to NFL success, and things can take time. Physical fronts like Texas A&M managed to expose his frame when attacking him head-on, while speed and counters from Virginia Tech and Florida highlighted pass sets that remain uneven rep-to-rep. Originally brought to college as a tackle, his future could lie within the interior where his instincts as a run blocker can shine without leaving him on an island against NFL edge-rushers.

For the Eagles, Okunlola is an extremely intriguing fit for a motion-heavy, wide-zone offense. Mannion's scheme requires interior linemen who carry weight comfortably without compromising lateral quickness allowing them to stretch perimeter run lanes and reach second-level linebackers, and Okunlola's movement and ability to work into angles could fit naturally within Philadelphia's front.

#2878. Luka Doncic scores 53 as Lakers avenge loss to Trail Blazers

The absurd 62-foot three-pointer Luka Doncic hoisted and made late in the second quarter Friday night seemed to ignite him, pushing him to even higher heights on the way to scoring 53 points.

Doncic especially went to work in the third quarter, scoring 20 points in that span in delivering the Lakers to a 124-118 win over the Portland Trail Blazers Friday night at Crypto.com Arena.

The Lakers had been down by 16 points in the second quarter, but after Doncic's shot, they trailed by only seven points and saw the momentum swing in the process.

"Oh yeah, definitely. I think it changes the whole energy in the team and in the gym," Doncic said. "I thought it was an important shot to make. So that's why I decided to make it."

It was the second time Doncic has scored 50 points or more this season, his first time coming when he scored 52 points against the Utah Jazz on Oct. 25. It was the fourth time Doncic has scored 50-plus points as a Laker, passing LeBron James and tying George Mikan for fourth in franchise regular-season history.

It was Doncic's 11th 50-plus point game of his career, which moved him pass Kareem Abdul-Jabbar and tied him with Allen Iverson for the 10th most in NBA history.

Lakers forward Jake LaRavia and Trail Blazers guard Scoot Henderson dive for a loose ball during the first half Friday night at Crypto.com Arena. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

Doncic played 38 minutes and nine seconds. He shot 17 for 33 from the field and five for 15 from three-point range.

At one point, while Doncic was talking to the media, he blurted out, "I'm tired."

A few minutes later, he was asked how he has been holding up during his offensive onslaught and how far he can keep pushing this.

"Feeling great," Doncic said. " But what do you mean how far?"

"Oh yeah, I feel like that," Doncic said. "But as long as we win, I'm good."

Doncic is now averaging 40.1 points per game this season.

He was asked if he knew the last player to average 40 points per game in a single season.

No, it was not Kobe Bryant, Doncic was told.

It was Wilt Chamberlain, who averaged 50.36 points per game during the 1961-62 season and then 44.83 points per game during the 62-63 season.

"Man, it's obviously a long season," Doncic said. "We're just 10 games in. We got 72 left. But as long as we win, I'm good. Got them up today, not gonna lie. Hopefully it continues, but you never know."

Doncic added five rebounds and five assists to help the Lakers win their first NBA Cup game after losing the first one to the Golden State Warriors last week.

When the Lakers saw their 16-point lead sliced to four in the fourth quarter, naturally it was Doncic who saved them.

His seven-foot floater with 27.7 seconds remaining gave the Lakers a six-point lead that stood.

Now everyone knows why Doncic is shooting a career-best from three-point range and why he practices those long-range threes all the time.

After Walker Kessler got a defensive rebound with 4.3 seconds left in the second quarter, he passed the ball to Doncic, who had nowhere to go with the Portland defense around him. So, Doncic just tossed up the three-pointer from the three-point line on the Portland side of the court that banked in as time expired. He looked at the crowd as he walked off the court for halftime.

"When he shoots them, it's not a surprise if it goes in," said Austin Reaves, who had 25 points. "So, it's not a high-percentage shot by any means, but he seems to make them a little, you know, easier than the majority of people."

Redick explained why both Bronny James and Cameron Carr were assigned to the Coachella Valley Lakers.

"[The] messaging was very simple," Redick said. "It's just you guys need reps and you guys need to play."

Redick said both James and Carr will be with the Lakers on the upcoming trip to Detroit, Atlanta, Milwaukee and Phoenix next week.

#2877. How new Eagles QB Joe Milton is preparing for unlikely first career start

How new Eagles QB Joe Milton is preparing for unlikely first career start originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

A few weeks ago, Joe Milton was in meetings and film study with Dak Prescott and former Eagle Sam Howell in the Cowboys' quarterback room at the team facility in Frisco, Texas.

Now he's the Eagles' starting quarterback.

"You never know what can happen in the NFL," Milton said after practice Thursday. "Yes, I was on the practice squad and I was just preparing for any moment that presents itself. Still, when I was at Dallas on the practice squad, I was just pretty much going through the plays, going through the week like I was a starter and just being ready.

"And that's what the NFL is about. Just being ready when your name is called and just preparing for those moments."

The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16, and on Sunday – 23 days later and after just 10 practices – he'll make his first NFL start against the Giants at the Linc.

"My coaches here, Parks (Frazier) and Sean (Mannion), did a great job of getting me caught up early," Milton said. "And I spent a lot of time inside the building just trying to learn this offense and the communication aspect of it.

"It's a lot of new terminology for me, so being in my third offense, just trying to plug and play and go from there. … Just executing whatever the play call is that comes through my helmet. Just call that play and execute. Know my job. Know my 1/11th. Do my 1/11th, and make a play."

Milton spent 2024 with the Patriots playing in Alex Van Pelt's offense then joined the Cowboys last year playing under Klayton Adams.

A lot of learning, a lot of new schemes, new coaches, new teammates. Just constant change for the 26-year-old Milton.

"It's just a whole new offense," Milton said. "I just came from my previous team, and a lot of stuff was just different. So coming here was just pretty much learning a whole new system of the West Coast and just going with that.

"Kind of go back to my rookie year, rookie year offense."

The Eagles were 2-3 this year under Jalen Hurts, now out for the season with blood clots, and 0-3 under Tanner McKee, benched this week for Milton.

This will be the first season since 2002 where the Eagles will start three different quarterbacks in meaningful games. In 2002, Donovan McNabb started the first 10 games, before breaking his right leg vs. the Cards at the Linc, Koy Detmer started vs. the 49ers at Candlestick but suffered a dislocated elbow and A.J. Feeley started the next five games before McNabb returned for the final two regular-season games and the playoffs.

Asked what people who haven't seen him play – which is pretty much everybody – should expect, Milton didn't talk about his mobility or his rocket-cannon arm, he just spoke about playing disciplined football within the scheme.

"Doing what the coach asks, going out there and whatever play is called, just going out there and executing that play, and nothing else matters in the world except for that play," he said. "So whatever it takes, whatever the team needs me to do, that's what I'm willing to do. If I have to run, if I have to throw or hand it off, it's perfect."

Milton hasn't started a game although he played most of a win against the Bills for the Patriots on the final day of the 2024 season, throwing for one touchdown and running for another. He got into four games with the Cowboys last year – all in mop-up duty late in blowout losses – and threw a touchdown and two INTs.

In parts of five games with the Patriots and Cowboys, he's completed 37 of 53 passes (70 percent) for 424 yards with two TDs and two INTs with a 90.4 passer rating.

Milton is the first quarterback who'll start for the Eagles after not being here on opening day since Ken O'Brien in 1993. He's the first former Cowboy to start at quarterback for the Eagles since Rodney Peete in 1995. He's the third NFL QB to make his first career start this year, following Jalon Daniels of the Buccaneers and Joe Fagnano of the Ravens.

"I approach the week the same," he said. "Came in here, worked hard and stayed late, got the plays like I always do for every week since I've been here, and it's been going good so far."

Milton said his 1-year-old daughter, Maleia, helps keep him grounded when things are kind of nutty.

"I mean, I always (want to) be where my feet are," he said. "My daughter does a great job of reminding me of that. Yes, she can't talk yet, but at the same time, when I see her face, it just allows me to be thankful. So when I see her every night on FaceTime, I'm just thankful. It allows me to take a deep breath and remember where I'm at. And being where my feet are is probably the best thing because you think too far and you never know what (can) happen."
