#2875. Gael Monfils appointed captain of France's Davis Cup team following retirement

Gael Monfils has been appointed France's Davis Cup captain a month after calling time on his playing career.

The flamboyant Frenchman, known for entertaining both on and off court, quietly retired in October - appearing on the International Tennis Integrity Agency's inactive list without an explanation - amid the breakdown of his marriage with Ukrainian player Elina Svitolina.

Having initially planned to bid farewell to the sport with one last appearance at the Paris Masters, he was instead honoured with a ceremony in the French capital earlier this week, joined by fellow fan favourites Jo-Wilfried Tsonga, Gilles Simon, Richard Gasquet and Benoit Paire.

He now transitions into a courtside role after succeeding Paul-Henri Mathieu as captain for France's upcoming Davis Cup campaign, with qualifying in February.

"It is a great source of pride to become captain of the French national team," Monfils said. "This blue jersey represents so much to me. I want to build a real collective project and create the dynamic that will allow us to win titles together."

The French Tennis Federation (FFT) thanked Mathieu for his commitment in the role for the past three years, having guided France to the quarter-finals in 2025.

The 40-year-old represented France in eight editions of the Davis Cup during his 22-year professional career, winning 12 of his 15 singles matches.

Gael Monfils retired from tennis last month and was given a rousing send-off in Paris (Reuters)

He delivered one of his best performances in the 2014 final, beating Roger Federer in straight sets, although France would ultimately go on to lose the tie to Switzerland.

The former World No 6 was not in the ‌team when France won their 10th Davis ​Cup title in 2017 but is now keen to ⁠add to their trophy cabinet as captain, ⁠a non-playing role.

The FFT says Monfils will be assisted by the likes of compatriots Simon and Gasquet in the role.

"We are very happy to welcome Gaël as head coach of the French team," FFT president Gilles Moretton added.

"We are convinced that his infectious energy and enthusiasm will be valuable assets in advancing the ambitions of French tennis."

#2873. Victor Wembanyama scores 30 as Spurs beat Kings for 7th straight win

SACRAMENTO, CA ― The San Antonio Spurs continue to roll, earning their seventh consecutive victory in taking down the Sacramento Kings, 124-109, in an NBA Cup group stage game on Friday, Nov. 6.

The Spurs were led by center Victor Wembanyama with 30 points on 10-of-18 shooting. He added 14 rebounds and two blocks. Four other players scored in double figures as Stephon Castle, Devin Vassell and Dylan Harper each had 19. Harrison Barnes scored 13 off the bench.

The Spurs dominated from the opening tip all the way to the end of the game. Wembanyama seems firmly in the early MVP race.

"I do not have a goal for Victor to get MVP," Spurs coach Mitch Johnson said before the game. "I think that there's a lot of things that we're trying to get better and improve on and execute that's gonna be a byproduct of the conversation that could produce."

"He has shown in a short amount of time, in his career, and season by season, however you want, compartmentalized time. His improvement is rapid," he added. "When he gets ahold of certain things, whether that's how he's being guarded, actions we want to run, teammates he's playing with, when he gets ahold of certain things, he can become very dominant. And if he continues to put his energy into the right stuff as he has, and to continue to have that level of retaining what works for him and works for us, then whatever the goals are, I would not bet against."

Spurs guard De'Aaron Fox was welcomed with a mix of boos and cheers as he was announced in the starting lineup. Fox was drafted by Sacramento with the No. 5 pick in the 2017 NBA Draft and spent eight seasons with the Kings before being traded to San Antonio. Fox got the first points on the board on Friday. He finished with nine.

Sacramento only led in the game for a brief moment early in the first quarter. Kings center Domantas Sabonis opened the game with a thunderous slam over Wembanyama. He finished with a double-double, 17 points and 12 rebounds, in the loss.

Kings dynamic scoring rookie Darius Acuff Jr. followed Sabonis' dunk with a 3-point field goal to put Sacramento ahead 5-2 with 10:45 left in the first period. Acuff picked up two early fouls, which sent him to the bench with 6:19 left after playing six minutes in the opening quarter. Acuff finished with 22 points and eight assists. He shot 9-of-20 from the field, including 3-of-9 on 3-point attempts.

The real disparity came with player fouls. The Kings committed 26 fouls as the Spurs shot 42 free throws.

"I mean, early on, it was good, but, I mean, you know, when you get to the free throw line as much as they were, that's tough," Kings head coach Doug Christie said. "Because not only are they a good defensive team, they're even better when their defense is set, and that's what the free throw allows you to do. When we were able to get in open court, we were able to find some things and create the type of 3-point shots and different things, collapse in the defense."

The Kings have back-to-back losses after getting their first win of the season against the Dallas Mavericks in a 115-100 finish on Nov. 2. Sacramento welcomes the Miami Heat on Sunday, Nov. 9 to the Golden 1 Center.

The Spurs won the first game of their four-game road trip. They immediately board a plane to face the Portland Trail Blazers on Saturday, Nov. 7.

#2872. Nick Sirianni emphatically backs OL coach Chris Kuper amid losing streak

Nick Sirianni emphatically backs OL coach Chris Kuper amid losing streak originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Chris Kuper was dealt a tough hand, but this is bad.

When the Eagles hired Kuper as their offensive line coach this offseason to replace the legendary Jeff Stoutland, the hope was that the Eagles would be healthy up front and return to dominance. That hasn't gone to plan.

And through eight games, the Eagles' struggling offensive line is one of the big reasons they are 2-6 and losers of six straight. But head coach Nick Sirianni still believes in Kuper. He gave an impassioned defense of his OL coach on Monday afternoon.

"Chris is coaching his [butt] off," Sirianni said on Monday. "He's fighting his [butt] off, he's coaching his [butt] off on a daily basis. You guys don't get to see that. I get it on a daily basis.

"You get to see that for three hours on Sunday, but he's coaching his [butt] off and he's fighting his [butt] off and I know he's giving the players good tools to use fundamentally and helping them be the best players that they possibly can be. But we all have to be better and we know that."

The next morning on 94WIP, Sirianni said more of the same about Kuper when asked about the way Stoutland departed the organization. He also said the Eagles' players have faith in Kuper and you can ask them. That might be true, but that can't matter more than results; and the results aren't good.

Through eight games, the Eagles are 30th in points and 32nd in total yards.

It's impossible to say if having Stoutland this season would have helped the Eagles greatly. But it's also impossible not to wonder, especially because his departure came after his role was diminished by Sirianni during the 2025 season. Stoutland is one of the greatest coaches in franchise history but even his line struggled last year.

It's also fair to point out that the line the Eagles have been using for much of the season is very different than what they expected entering the year. At the start of the season, the Eagles were banking on Lane Johnson, Landon Dickerson and Cam Jurgens. Well, Johnson retired after playing in three games, Jurgens was traded to the Ravens and Dickerson has played just 3 of 8 games.

Here's a look at the Eagles' OL throughout this season:

Week 1: Jordan Mailata, Landon Dickerson, Cam Jurgens, Tyler Steen, Lane JohnsonWeek 2: Jordan Mailata, Cam Jurgens, Drew Kendall, Tyler Steen, Lane JohnsonWeek 3: Jordan Mailata, Cam Jurgens, Drew Kendall, Tyler Steen, Lane JohnsonWeek 4: Jordan Mailata, Cam Jurgens, Drew Kendall, Tyler Steen, Markel BellWeek 5: Jordan Mailata, Willie Lampkin, Jake Majors, Tyler Steen, Markel BellWeek 6: Jordan Mailata, Willie Lampkin, Jake Majors, Tyler Steen, Markel BellWeek 7: Jordan Mailata, Landon Dickerson, Drew Kendall, Tyler Steen Markel BellWeek 8: Jordan Mailata, Landon Dickerson, Drew Kendall, Tyler Steen Markel Bell

In addition to the constant state of flux on the line, the Eagles have dealt with a ton of injuries to other players, including Jalen Hurts, DeVonta Smith, Saquon Barkley and more.

So it wasn't reasonable to expect the Eagles' offensive line to completely save them. But it's not unreasonable to expect better execution when it comes to things like assignments and communication. The Eagles have struggled with simple things up front at times this year. The Eagles have the 27th-ranked rushing offense in the NFL in 2026 and have given up a pressure rate of 42.65, which ranks 29th.

And the Eagles have been absolutely flummoxed by blitzes all year. They entered Week 8 allowing the highest pressure rate on blitzes in the NFL this season (57.7%), per NextGen Stats, and were even worse against the Commanders on Sunday Night Football. The Commanders generated 10 pressures on 12 blitzes (83.3%), which is the second-highest pressure rate on blitzes by a team in the 2026 season.

The Eagles have now faced 109 blitzes this season and have given up 66 pressures — an outrageous rate of 60.6% The Eagles have also allowed a free rusher on 18.2% of their passing plays this season, the most of any team, according to Ryan Paganetti.

A big focus for the offense this week is to fix the communication errors that have plagued them through eight weeks. Sean Mannion also expressed his confidence in Kuper this week.

"I think Koop's been doing an outstanding job," Mannion said. "I know, like I said, everything ultimately starts with me and making sure I'm charting the right course for us in terms of our protection plan, in terms of how we're getting it taught, in terms of our install meetings and everything like that. But Chris is doing a great job. He's a tremendous teacher of the fundamentals. He's great with those guys in the room."

Kuper was not retained by the Vikings after the 2025 season and the Eagles hired him with the hope that his knowledge in this system would help the transition to Mannion. Nothing on offense has worked this year.

So there's no doubt that the pressure on Kuper has started to get ratcheted up in recent weeks as the Eagles' struggles continue. But the Eagles seem like they're sticking with him for now.

There are a lot of problems with the Eagles' offense this season, as Sirianni is happy to share.

"Again, we all have to do better," he said. "I think, again, we want to zero in on one thing. We're not coaching well enough right now and we're not playing well enough right now. I know we're speaking offensively and right now we're not doing all the things we need to do to win football games, and that falls on everyone's shoulders. It's not just one thing. We have to dig deep as coaches, as players, and again, find solutions, find answers."

#2870. Ravens updated QB depth chart after Jacoby Brissett trade

The Baltimore Ravens have a new starting quarterback, and the team's depth chart has changed again after officially acquiring Jacoby Brissett from the Arizona Cardinals.

Baltimore finalized the trade Friday, receiving Brissett and a 2027 fifth-round draft pick in exchange for a 2027 fourth-round selection and a 2028 seventh-round pick. The Ravens subsequently waived quarterback Austin Reed, who served as Joe Fagnano's backup during Thursday night's 33-10 loss to the Jacksonville Jaguars.

Brissett is expected to become Baltimore's fourth different starting quarterback in four consecutive games when the Ravens host the Los Angeles Chargers on Monday, Nov. 16.

Here's how Baltimore's quarterback depth chart projects following the Brissett trade and Reed's release:

Jacoby Brissett — QB1: Expected starter against the Chargers.

Joe Fagnano — QB2: Undrafted rookie who started Thursday against Jacksonville.

Kedon Slovis — QB3: Acquired from Green Bay's practice squad earlier this week.

Lamar Jackson — Injured reserve: Recovering from a right thumb injury that required surgery.

Tyler Huntley — Injured reserve: Dealing with a toe injury reportedly expected to end his season.

The backup order behind Brissett is projected and has not been formally announced by Minter.

Brissett immediately becomes Baltimore's most experienced available quarterback, bringing 73 career starts over 11 NFL seasons. The 33-year-old started Arizona's first eight games this season, completing 63.3% of his passes for 1,719 yards, 10 touchdowns and seven interceptions before losing his starting job.

His experience should provide stability for an offense that has struggled to produce consistently without Jackson.

Baltimore has been held below 14 points in four consecutive games for the first time in franchise history, and Thursday's loss illustrated the limitations of its inexperienced quarterback room. Brissett will have more than a week to become familiar with Doyle's offense and establish timing with Zay Flowers, Rashod Bateman and Mark Andrews.

Fagnano started Thursday against Jacksonville but struggled throughout the 33-10 defeat, completing 10 of 18 passes for 60 yards and two interceptions. He finished with a 22.7 passer rating as Baltimore managed just 55 net passing yards.

The former UConn quarterback remains an option behind Brissett, though the coaching staff must decide whether his familiarity with the offense gives him an edge over Slovis. Baltimore signed Slovis from Green Bay's practice squad Nov. 3, adding another quarterback after Jackson and Huntley suffered injuries against Buffalo.

Reed's departure further clarifies the quarterback rotation. He was promoted from the practice squad Thursday and served as Fagnano's backup against Jacksonville but did not play.

Jackson and Huntley were placed on injured reserve Thursday, making both quarterbacks ineligible to return for at least four games. Jackson's recovery timeline remains uncertain following his thumb injury. Baltimore has not ruled out a potential return later this season, although there is no guarantee the two-time NFL MVP will play again in 2026.

Huntley's injury is reportedly season-ending, leaving Brissett, Fagnano and Slovis as Baltimore's available quarterbacks.

The Ravens enter Week 10 at 4-5, riding a four-game losing streak. Brissett's arrival won't automatically fix an inconsistent offensive line or Baltimore's problems sustaining drives, but it gives the organization a veteran quarterback who can run the offense as the team tries to keep its playoff hopes alive.

#2869. Why Joe Mazzulla hugged Neemias Queta instead of yelling at him in win over Hornets

CHARLOTTE — Early in the third quarter of the Celtics' 119-80 win over the Charlotte Hornets, Joe Mazzulla called a timeout and walked over to Neemias Queta.

Queta had just gotten tangled up with multiple Hornets players while trying to secure a rebound. And, as is often the case, Mazzulla grew frustrated with his big man's longstanding tendency to foul.

But this time, instead of reaming him out, Mazzulla chose to embrace him.

"I'm just trying to channel my anger into love," Mazzulla said afterward.

"Instead of just yelling at him, I gave him a hug."

It's no secret that Mazzulla is hard on the Portuguese big man, who began his journey with the Celtics on a two-way contract in 2023 and blossomed into the Celtics' productive and reliable starting center last season.

Sometimes, Mazzulla's yelling gets picked up by the TV broadcast. More often than not, his anger is visible from press row.

And, none of that tough love is something that the Celtics head coach denies.

"I'm hard on Neemi," Mazzulla said on Friday night. "I'm hard on our bigs. Those guys, the way they need to play for us, there's a ton of pressure on them. We talk about it all the time — the fives have the toughest job on this team because they got to do a ton on both ends of the floor."

Mazzulla has frequently discussed how difficult the center position is in the team's system, particularly as of late. That's also applied to Mitchell Robinson, who has struggled in his acclimation to the Celtics as he takes on more responsibility in a new system.

Still, Mazzulla wants to make sure that amid his tough coaches style, there's an understanding that all of it is rooted in love. He visits players in the offseason in order to better understand what makes them tick; this past summer, he joined Queta in Portugal, Mitchell Robinson in Nashville, and Luka Garza in Iowa.

In difficult moments, there's a relationship that runs deeper than basketball.

"I appreciate that they allow me to be hard on them, but I also want them to know I appreciate them," he said. "Just trying to do that for them."

Queta is not the only player to get a hug from Joe Mazzulla as of late; Payton Pritchard got an in-game hug from Mazzulla in a win over the Chicago Bulls, after a couple of high-impact plays.

"Joe challenged me," Pritchard said after that win. "I feel like the first four games my defensive activity and my rebounding have been poor… He's challenging me to step up in that area. I think the hug was for me doing that."

On Friday, Pritchard reflected on how Mazzulla has successfully tapped into both sides of the emotional spectrum as a coach.

"Joe, he's a competitor, and he has a lot of emotion, so it brings the best out of us, and it's also cool when you are doing well; he shows the emotion of a loving brother type situation," Pritchard said. "And then we'll have moments where we get into it, and we have disagreements, and it's all right. It's just part of it — we go through love and hate."

Pritchard said his bond with Mazzulla has strengthened over the years; he's been playing for him since he took over as head coach in 2022, and it's come with plenty of variance. Pritchard has gone through stretches in which he's fallen out of the rotation, won Sixth Man of the Year, and was a part of the 2024 championship team.

"It's a journey," he said. "We've definitely gone through a lot of different situations, a lot of ups and downs, and you just grow your relationship through that, and it becomes stronger and stronger."

Mazuzlla echoed that sentiment last week, after his hug.

"I like pushing him, I like coaching him, and I love him like a brother," Mazzulla said of Pritchard.

For Mazzulla, turning anger into love has been the result of an intentional effort, especially when it comes to his relationship with Queta.

"A lot of meditation, a lot of journaling, a lot of just not being so mad at him all the time," Mazzulla said. "But I love him."

Still, Mazzulla's ability to leverage anger hasn't gone away, the Celtics' veterans say.

"It just depends on the day," said Jayson Tatum with a smile. "Some good days, some bad days."

"They're both needed," Pritchard said. "You can't have love without the anger."

#2867. GAME THREAD: Georgetown vs Maryland

Game 2: Georgetown Hoyas (1-0) vs Maryland Terrapins (1-0)When: Saturday 7:30 PMWhere: Capital One ArenaTV: Peacock, NBC Sports YouTube Channel (Mike Corey and Nick Bahe) Radio: Bloomberg 99.1 (Rich Chvotkin), Sirius XM 964 (Georgetown)

Series: Georgetown trails 38-30 overall but leads 17-14 at homeLast Meeting: Georgetown beat Maryland 70-60 last season on the road (Nov. 7, 2025)Streaks: Georgetown ended a two-game losing streak to the Terps with last season's victory

KenPom Rankings: Maryland 52, Georgetown 84KenPom Odds: Maryland has a 55% win probability, with a predicted score of 74-73DraftKings Line: Georgetown +2.5, o/u 145.5 ML +130

Buzz Williams begins his second season in College Park after a rough 12-21 campaign. Maryland was picked to finish 12th in the 18-team Big 10 preseason poll. In their season opening win over Bucknell the Terps had five in double figures – Andre Mills (returning), Kaden House (freshman), Pharrel Payne (returning), D.J. Wagner (Arkansas), and Tomislav Buljan (New Mexico). Much hyped local freshman Baba Oladotun (Silver Spring) scored seven points in 19 minutes off the bench. Oladotun chose Maryland over Georgetown.

WHAT'S AT STAKE?Local bragging rights. A solid non-conference win on the resume come March. Maybe winning over some local fans? It goes without saying that this is a pretty big game for two teams that struggled last season.

Georgetown will try and beat Maryland in consecutive games for the first time since the 1979-80 season when the Hoyas took down the Terps in December and then again in the NCAA Tournament. Saturday's game will be just the seventh meeting since 1980, with two more scheduled to follow.

Hoyas coach Ed Cooley gave his thoughts on the matchup during a Thursday media availability.

"I think it's early in the process to call it a rivalry. It probably should evolve to that given the proximity of the universities. It's something that we hope can turn into that. It's a game that both teams desperately have to try and win. To me, when the selection committee looks at it sometime in March, this will be one of the games—if we're fortunate, if they're fortunate to be an at large team—if we don't win our respective conference championships. It's one of those games again long winded answer to eventually evolve into something that we can call a rivalry game."

"We need a home court advantage as much as we can," Cooley said about Georgetown's homecourt situation.

"We play in one of the largest arenas in college basketball here in Washington which is a plus sometimes but it's also a negative if we don't have enough people in there making enough noise and energy to give us that extra boost when we need it."

Cooley confirmed that Georgetown will be without Vyctorious Miller (Oklahoma State).

#2866. Eagles snap counts: Time to see more of Eli Stowers

Eagles snap counts: Time to see more of Eli Stowers  originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles have lost six games in a row and you're probably tired of watching Johnny Mundt and Grant Calcaterra play football.

That's probably a big harsh but the Eagles have now lost six games in a row and they're simply not going to recover this season. It's time to see what some younger players can do and Eli Stowers is right at the top of that list.

Here's how the Eagles' tight end snaps broke down in Sunday night's 27-10 loss to the Commanders:

Dallas Goedert: 55 snaps (80%)Johnny Mundt: 13 snaps (19%)Eli Stowers: 13 snaps (19%)Grant Calcaterra: 12 snaps (19%)

The Eagles signed Mundt to a one-year deal this offseason with the promise that he would be an ideal blocking tight end. Howie Roseman talked about needing to find that element for the tight end room and Mundt was supposed to be that guy. It hasn't worked. So even though Stowers doesn't fit that mold — he's more of an athletic pass catcher — it's time to see more of him.

In his second NFL game, Stowers caught his first NFL pass. It was a 7-yarder in the third quarter to move the chains on a 2nd-6. He also caught a couple passes in the final moments of the fourth quarter to give him 3 for 53 against the Commanders. In a season like this it becomes important to develop young talent. Stowers had 12 snaps in his debut and 13 in his second game; the Eagles should continue to increase his playing time.

• Tanner McKee and his entire offensive line played all 69 snaps against the Commanders. McKee was under pressure all night and was sacked five times. The blitz kept getting home.

• Without DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks, the Eagles were obviously short-handed at receiver. Here's how the snaps broke down: Darius Cooper – 66 snaps (96%), Makai Lemon – 65 snaps (94%), Hollywood Brown – 32 snaps (46%), Elijah Moore – 18 snaps (26%). Lemon did some good things with 6 catches on 9 targets for 57 yards.

• Saquon Barkley played through hamstring and back injuries and got a total of 22 snaps (32%). He was bottled up way more in this game than he was a week ago against Dallas. Elijah Mitchell led the running back room with 39 snaps (57%) and Jaydon Blue got on the field for 8 snaps (12%).

• Willie Lampkin and Fred Johnson were on the field for that Barkley direct-snap touchdown in the first quarter. Lampkin lined up as a lead blocker.

• Cooper DeJean, Zack Baun and Quinyon Mitchell each played all 60 defensive snaps. Drew Mukuba (59) and Riq Woolen (58) were right behind them. It was another forgettable performance by the defense but at least Mukuba made a couple plays. He forced a fumble and had an interception that was negated because of a penalty.

• Without Jihaad Campbell (hamstring/quad), Jeremiah Trotter Jr. played 54 of 60 snaps (90%) and led the Eagles in tackles with 10.

• The DT rotation: Jalen Carter – 49 snaps (82%), Jordan Davis – 37 snaps (62%), Moro Ojomo – 36 snaps (60%), Byron Young – 15 snaps (25%), Ty Robinson – 9 snaps (15%).

• The edge rotation: Nolan Smith – 47 snaps (78%), Jonathan Greenard – 28 snaps (47%), Arnold Ebiketie – 24 snaps (40%), Jalyx Hunt – 21 snaps (35%). Smith returned from a concussion and at least provided some juice.

• Kenny Moore played 29 snaps but got banged up in the game. Marcus Epps ended up playing 13 snaps.

#2865. Brandon Aiyuk: I’m back to 100 percent and looking to get reinstated

Brandon Aiyuk will not play for the 49ers again, but his future is in question.

The wide receiver arrived at the team’s business offices in a van with his agent Friday afternoon to meet with the 49ers and presumably negotiate a resolution.

“Obviously, Brandon is here to meet with the team,” Drew Rosenhaus, Aiyuk’s agent, said before the meeting, via video from David Lombardi of the San Francisco Standard. “Fortunately, he’s feeling good physically. He’s healthy. He’s ready to get back to work. Don’t really have any comment about where he’s going to play. We’ll keep that between us and the Niners. But obviously we’re here with all the right intentions.”

Aiyuk last played in a game on Oct. 20, 2024, when he tore the ACL and MCL in his right knee. The relationship between the team and the player fell apart after that, and the 49ers voided the guaranteed money due to his unwillingness to rehab at their facility.

The 49ers placed him on the reserve/left squad list on Dec. 13, 2025. Rosenhaus filed a reinstatement request on Oct. 27, but the NFL still hasn’t reinstated him.

“Nice to see you all,” Aiyuk said. “I’m feeling great, feeling healthy. It was a long recovery process, a long rehab process. But I’m back to 100 percent and looking to get reinstated into the National Football League.”

49ers coach Kyle Shanahan said earlier in the day he "[couldn’t] care less” how the meeting went since “it’s been over for our football team for a long time.”

#2863. Gaël Monfils stays in tennis as captain of France's Davis Cup team

PARIS (AP) — Gaël Monfils will stay in tennis as captain of France's Davis Cup team, the national federation said Saturday.

The 40-year-old Monfils retired last month as one of the most popular personalities in the sport among fans and players. He had been expected to finish his career at the Paris Masters this week until it was announced that his marriage to Ukrainian star Elina Svitolina was ending.

He played in the 2014 Davis Cup final and beat Roger Federer to win France's only point in a 3-1 loss at home to Switzerland.

"It's a huge pride to become captain of the team," Monfils said in a statement. "This blue shirt means so much to me."

France will start its next Davis Cup campaign in February, with Monfils assisted by former players Gilles Simon and Richard Gasquet, the federation said.

Ten-time champion France last won the Davis Cup in 2017.

#2862. Player Grades: Lakers vs. Blazers

For so long in the LeBron James-Anthony Davis era, the Lakers were searching for 3-and-D wings to put around their stars.

One of the best parts of this current Lakers team is the dearth of wings. Not only does it allow them to have multiple of them on the floor throughout the game, but it also affords them options depending on who is playing well.

On Friday, for example, Matisse Thybulle, who has been a consistent contributor this season, struggled. The Lakers were able to turn to Jake LaRavia, then Ziaire Williams and even debuted Adou Thiero as well. It's such a luxury that this team hasn't had for years, allowing them to pick and choose the best groupings each night.

#2859. Roob's Eagles Observations: Life without Jalen Hurts just isn't any fun

Roob's Eagles Observations: Life without Jalen Hurts just isn't any fun originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Mulling life without Jalen Hurts. Thoughts on Tanner McKee getting benched after just three games. And the absurdity of something that the Eagles have done for the first time since 1938.

We've never had to do 10 Random Eagles Observations during a six-game losing streak before, but here we are.

MISSING JALEN HURTS: I miss Jalen Hurts. And I hope people out there who never became Hurts supporters for whatever reason even after all the wins and and all the big plays and five straight playoff berths, two Super Bowl appearances, a championship and a parade two years ago miss him, too. Since 2021, when Hurts plays the Eagles win 70 percent of the time and when he doesn't play they win 30 percent of the time. Love him or hate him that 70 percent figure is pretty impressive. Patrick Mahomes is the only NFL quarterback to win more games than Hurts and win a Super Bowl since 2021. No, Hurts is not perfect. He's human and he makes mistakes and sometimes he even loses games – but just 25 of them since 2021. But, dang, he makes things fun and exciting and gives this team a heck of a good chance to win football games when he's on the field. He's special, and right now there's not a lot special about the Philadelphia Eagles. I don't know exactly what this season would have looked like if Hurts hadn't been sidelined. There still would have been issues with the defense and the running game and pass protection. But one thing I do know is that Hurts raises the level of everyone around him and when he's not playing the Eagles miss more than just his quarterback play. They miss his leadership, his accountability, his presence. There are a lot of unknowns with Hurts' medical condition. I just hope he's able to make a complete healthy recovery and first, that he can live a healthy life with his family but second, that he's able to return to play quarterback for the Eagles in 2027. Because it's just not nearly as much fun without him.

AT LEAST THERE'S ONE GUY PLAYING WELL: Makai Lemon is on pace for 65 catches, and the Eagles' record for rookie wide receivers is 67 by Jordan Matthews in 2014. Tight end Keith Jackson caught 81 passes as a rookie, but Lemon has 31 receptions and needs 37 in Eagles' the last nine games to break Matthews' record. That's just over four per game, and based on the growth we've seen from him over the last few weeks there's no doubt in my mind he'll get that record and finish somewhere in the 70s. Which is truly impressive considering he missed much of training camp with a hamstring and got off to a slow start once the season began, with just 10 catches for 65 yards in his first four games. He has 21-for-186 the last four games and is really looking comfortable out there, catching the ball smoothly, breaking tackles, picking up yards after the catch and blocking in the run game. At some point, DeVonta Smith will be back from his own hamstring injury, and Dontayvion Wicks will be back as well. But the Eagles can't let Lemon's snap count drop. He plays mainly in the slot – 66 percent of his snaps are inside so far this year – and Smith is outside 88 percent of the time and Wicks is outside 72 percent of the time so Lemon shouldn't be affected. But the kid has to play. Not a lot to be excited about with this miserable offense, but Lemon is the real deal, and he's only going to keep getting better.

A QUICK HOOK FOR TANNER #1: I didn't think Nick Sirianni would pull the plug on Tanner McKee this quickly, especially with the bye week coming up. That's normally when you make these sort of changes. Especially with a new guy who's still learning the offense. But at this point, why not? You're 2-6, you've lost six straight, you're certainly not going anywhere with McKee at QB, what do you have to lose? McKee has ranged from poor to mediocre in his three starts and just never got into any sort of rhythm as a passer. It's a tough evaluation because the o-line has been so bad, the running game has never really gotten going and he's been without DeVonta Smith and to a large degree Saquon Barkley. And Joe Milton will be facing the same handicaps as McKee did, but the hope is that he can rise up above all those handicaps with his athleticism and provide some juice to the NFL's worst offense. McKee has just been lifeless. Out of 38 quarterbacks who've thrown at least 50 passes this year, he's 33rd in passer rating, 37th in yards per attempt 37th in yards per completion and 33rd in success rate. His four completions of at least 20 yards the last three weeks are 24th-most in the league. One more than Daniel Jones. Maybe it would have gotten better, but I haven't seen any reason to believe that or expect that. McKee has just looked overmatched. It seems awfully early for Milton, but he can't be any worse. Right?

A QUICK HOOK FOR TANNER #2: McKee lost more games this year than Jalen Hurts lost in 2022 and as many as Jalen lost in 2024.

A QUICK HOOK FOR TANNER #3: If McKee doesn't start another game, he'll be the first Eagles quarterback to start at least three games in a season and lose them all since Ken O'Brien went 0-4 in 1993. The only other QB to start three or more games (not including 1987 strike replacement games) and lose them all was John Reaves, who was 0-7 in 1972. During the strike, replacement QB Scott Tinsley went 0-3.

HOW CAN NONE OF THEM HAVE A SACK? It's hard to believe Jalyx Hunt, Moro Ojomo, Jonathan Greenard and Jordan Davis have a combined half of a sack through nearly half the season. Over the last two years, those four combined for 35 sacks. Now they have a half. Like not even an integer. I don't understand how an entire defensive line can go into a slump at the same time. Jalen Carter has four sacks, but it feels like he's making a few impressive plays a game but without the consistent production the Eagles need from him. Carter still gets a lot of double teams and Ojomo is also getting doubled a lot, and when anybody up front gets doubled it should be an opportunity for someone else to take advantage of a good matchup, but other than with Nolan Smith, who has four sacks of his own, that just hasn't happened. Ten sacks in eight games is pitiful. The Eagles once sacked Troy AIkman 11 times in one game. In 1989 they had 36 sacks after eight games. Now they have 10. Their 2nd-fewest ever after nine games, one more than the 1993 team had. I expected the offense to have issues this year. I did not expect the defense, especially the defensive line, to underachieve like this.

THIS REALLY OUGHT TO BE IMPOSSIBLE: Maybe the most damning stat regarding the 2026 Eagles is that they've been outgained by an average of over 100 yards per game. How is that even possible? Through Week 8, the Eagles have allowed 2,938 yards and netted 2,122 yards for a difference of 816 yards – or 102 yards per game. That's what happens when you're ranked 32nd in the NFL in yards per game and 27th in yards allowed. This is the first time since 1971 – that was 55 years ago – the Eagles have been outgained by at least 100 yards per game through eight games. Also happened in 1937, 1938 and 1957. The 1930s were not good years for the Eagles. Neither is 2026. The Eagles have been outgained by at least 71 yards in each game during the six-game losing streak. They've been outgained by an average of 148 yards during these last six games, the most yards they've been outgained by in any six-game span since 1976. This is only the second time in franchise history they've been outgained by 70 or more yards in six games in a row. It also happened during the 2023 late-season collapse. Fun season.

WHAT WAS THE QB PLAN? I feel like the Eagles have mis-managed the quarterback position since camp began, starting with the wonky competition between Andy Dalton and Tanner McKee during training camp, when neither one got enough reps to be ready for the season. And then going with Dalton as No. 2 but demoting him after just one pass? And alienating him so much that he opted to sign with the Falcons instead of returning here after Jalen Hurts was sidelined for the season? Then a quick hook for McKee to go with a QB who was drafted in the sixth round, just got here 3 ½ weeks ago and has never started an NFL game? Jalen wasn't playing great when his season ended, McKee regressed, Dalton threw the worst pass of the season and Cole Payton isn't ready. Maybe Milton will play well Sunday, but I don't know how you start training camp with four quarterbacks on the roster and go into a Week 10 home game against a division rival with someone else as your starter.

CAN THEY MAKE IT FOUR IN A ROW? With Joe Milton making his first NFL start Sunday, it's interesting to note that the last three Eagles quarterbacks to make their first NFL start all won. That's Carson Wentz in the 2016 opener against the Browns, Jalen Hurts against the Saints in Week 14 of the 2020 season after replacing Wentz and Tanner McKee in the meaningless year-end game against the Giants in 2024. The last Eagles QBs to lose their first career start are Kevin Kolb in 2009 against the Saints and Nick Foles in 2012 in Washington. Before that, Donovan McNabb won his first start in 1999 after replacing Doug Pederson, at the Vet vs. Washington, and A.J. Feeley won his first start late in 2002 against the Cards at the Vet. So Eagles QBs making their first career start are 5-2 in their last seven games. Before that? Eagles QBs making their first start were 3-9-1.

THEY REALLY NEED A HEALTHY SAQUON: With Dameon Pearce averaging 2.6 yards per carry, Tank Bigsby at 3.3, Will Shipley 2.9 and Elijah Mitchell 3.2, the Eagles could have four running backs average less than 3.4 yards per carry in the same season for the first time since 1991, when James Joseph averaged 3.3, Heath Sherman 2.6, Thomas Sanders 2.3 and Robert Drummond 2.3. Just another thing to look forward to over these last nine weeks.

HOW MANY GAMES ARE THEY GOING TO WIN? As the Eagles head into the second half of a lost season, it's interesting to look back at the biggest drops in wins in franchise history from one season to the next. The Eagles won 11 games last year and I'm not sure how many more they'll win, but it won't be a lot. But what's crazy is that the Eagles haven't had a dropoff of more than four wins in more than 20 years. They won 13 games in the 2004 Super Bowl season, then went 6-10 in 2005. Not including the 1982 strike-shortened season, the last time before that they won four fewer games from one season to the next was 1971 (6-7-1) to 1972 (2-11-1). The seven-game dropoff from 2004 through 2005 matches the largest in franchise history. They also won seven fewer games from 1961 to 1962 (10-4, 3-10-1). They'll need to find a way to win three more games to avoid tying the largest single-season dropoff in wins in the franchise's 94-year history, and I'm not sure they can.

FIRST TIME SINCE 1938? And this is just astounding: The Eagles have been out-rushed by 350 yards and out-passed by 466 yards so far this year. This is the first time in 88 years they've been outgained by at least 350 yards both on the ground and in the air eight games into a season. It also happened in 1937 (649 rushing, 360 passing) and 1938 (517 rushing, 538 passing). You didn't want to be an Eagles fan in the 1930s.

#2857. Fixtures and standings as Nations Championship returns

The Nations Championship resumed on Friday with Ireland securing a 42-12 win over Argentina, as the battle for continental supremacy heats up.

After three rounds of home games for southern hemisphere nations in July, the northern hemisphere teams are the hosts for the final three rounds.

Ireland's win over Argentina moved them top of the standings in the north, but France and Scotland can move above the Irish with victories on Saturday.

Six Nations champions France face Fiji on Saturday while Scotland - who are one point behind Les Bleus - hope to beat against New Zealand for the first time at Murrayfield on Saturday before games against Australia and Japan.

Third-placed England have 10 points and play Australia - who are third in the southern standings - at Allianz Stadium on Sunday before taking on Japan and New Zealand.

Ireland, who also have 10 points, face the challenge of taking on South Africa in the final round after playing Fiji in their second home game.

Wales beat Fiji before losing to Argentina and South Africa in their opening three matches. Their home games are against Japan, New Zealand and Australia.

Bottom-side Italy lost all three of their away games and face South Africa on Saturday.

The Springboks beat England, Scotland and Wales at home in their opening three Tests to lead New Zealand on points difference.

Player-coach rift report will only galvanise England - George

Scotland come full circle for another crack at All Blacks

Japan date starts pivotal month for Welsh rugby

Townsend never felt he could beat All Blacks as a player, but as a coach...

How are Ireland set for the Nations Championship?

How will Wales fare on Nations Championship return?

The Nations Championship pits the northern hemisphere teams who compete in the Six Nations - England, Ireland, Scotland, Wales, France and Italy - against southern hemisphere giants South Africa, New Zealand, Australia and Argentina, plus invited sides Fiji and Japan.

All teams will play each of the six in the opposing hemisphere once, with three rounds of fixtures staged in July and another three in November.

Their results will rank the teams within their own hemisphere, from one to six.

On the final weekend in November, there is a three-day play-off event staged at Allianz Stadium in Twickenham. The sixth-placed team in the northern hemisphere plays off against the equivalent in the southern hemisphere standings and so on, culminating in the two top-ranked sides taking each other on.

The winner of that final match is crowned the inaugural Nations Championship winner.

There is also a parallel, Ryder-Cup style hemisphere title. The winner of each of the matches on the play-offs weekend will earn one point for their hemisphere, except for the contest between the two top-ranked teams which delivers two.

The first hemisphere to earn four points over the weekend will be crowned winners.

The Nations Championship trophy and the shield awarded to the best hemisphere are pictured at the Tower of London in London [Getty Images]

Third-place North v Third-place South (20:10 GMT)

Fifth-place North v Fifth-place South (13:10 GMT)

Second-place North v Second-place South (16:40 GMT)

Fourth-place North v Fourth-place South (13:10 GMT)

First-place North v First-place South (16:40 GMT)

#2856. In defending his willingness to spend, Jerry Jones may have admitted to tampering

On Friday, Cowboys owner Jerry Jones tried to reverse the perception/reality that he deliberately underspends on coaches. His explanation regarding the willingness to spend included an important admission.

“I have been trying to lure very successful coaches away from the teams they were on,” Jones said on 105.3 The Fan in Dallas.

Unless he was referring to college coaches (or high-school coaches), that’s tampering. Period. End of discussion.

It could be false — an effort to bolster the idea that, yes, he’s willing to spend. Still, the effort to put out the frugality fire he started on Tuesday by ridiculously claiming there’s no correlation between coaching pay and winning included a nonchalant concession that he has tried to pry NFL coaches away from their current teams, in blatant violation of the rules.

Will the NFL look into this one? Probably not. Because the league office surely realizes, as the rest of us do, that Jerry was simply saying whatever he had to say to lessen the impact of the accidental truth he told on Tuesday regarding a deliberate reluctance to do whatever he needs to do to make his team as good as it can be.

#2854. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

#2853. Eagles notebook: What will the future hold for Drew Mukuba?

Eagles notebook: What will the future hold for Drew Mukuba? originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Vic Fangio doesn't just dish out compliments so what he said about Drew Mukuba this week really stood out.

What has Fangio made of Mukuba's season so far?

"Overall good," Fangio said. "Again, some plays that he'd like to have back, but I think he's a good young player that's going to be a good player in this league for a long time."

For Fangio, that's high praise. Mukuba is a second-year safety who has struggled at times this season. But Fangio's saying he's going to be a good player in the league for a long time didn't go unnoticed.

"It just means that he has a lot of confidence and belief in me," Mukuba said. "I've got to just feed off of that and keep building."

The entire Eagles defense has struggled this season and Mukuba has been a part of that. But the 2025 second-round pick had perhaps his best game of the season in Week 8 against the Commanders when he forced a fumble and had an interception that was negated because of a penalty.

Through nine weeks, Mukuba has one interception and one forced fumble. Getting takeaways is a big part of his game and if Mukuba is going to live up to his potential, he needs to get more.

"I feel like that's why I play safety, is to get the ball back," he said. "I feel like that's why they brought me here is to make plays on the ball. That's my job and I need to do a better job doing that."

When asked about which part of his game needs to improve most, Mukuba thought about giving a general answer and saying everything, but then he got honest: His tackling.

Mukuba this season, according to PFF, has missed 11 tackles. Just four players in the NFL have missed more. Mukuba said his issues are more about bad angles than they are about wrapping up. He said practice reps are the only thing that will help him improve there.

And Mukuba has also gotten beat in coverage this season. While defensive back coverage stats are generally pretty unreliable, especially for teams who use a lot of zone, Mukuba's coverage numbers are not good. He has given up 15 catches on 17 targets for 2 TDs and 1 INT this season. His opponent passer rating is 133.5.

But Mukuba is still a young player in the NFL. And because he is a second-round pick last year, the Eagles are obviously hoping he can become a long-time starter at the safety position.

"I feel like I'm getting better every game," Mukuba said. "I feel like I'm playing my best ball with more left in me."

Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts was back in the team facility this week and was able to sit in on meetings, ESPN's Tim McManus first reported on Wednesday.

"Jalen's great. Obviously a great leader," Tanner McKee said. "It was fun seeing him again. Talking to somebody on the phone, it's not the same as seeing him in person. Definitely lifted the mood. It was really great seeing and talking to him in person."

Hurts, 28, is going to miss the rest of the season with blood clots in his lungs. But just having him around as a leader will mean something to his teammates. On Wednesday, head coach Nick Sirianni was asked how much he has talked to Hurts.

"Of course, I've seen him, and I have no new update on anything," Sirianni said. "But of course, I've talked to him and am in constant communication with him and know he's resting and recovering. I miss him like crazy. But nothing new on that front.

"I know he's resting and recovering, and we're all still praying for him and hoping for a speedy recovery."

There are a lot of numbers to illustrate just how bad the Eagles have been this season, but they're even very bad in the metrics that Sirianni always talks about. Since he was hired in 2021, Sirianni has always stressed two things: The turnover battle and the explosive play battle.

• Through eight games, the Eagles have just four total takeaways and have turned the ball over eight times. That's a turnover differential of -4. Just four teams have a worse turnover differential this season. This is their worst turnover differential through eight games in the Nick Sirianni Era.

2022: +152025: +42021: +12024: -22023: -32024: -4

• The other thing Sirianni tracks are explosive plays. The Eagles under Nick Sirianni have always defined explosive plays as 10+ yard runs and 16+ yard passes. They have won that battle just once this season and through eight games they're -24 in explosive plays.

The Eagles had held the Commanders to under 100 rushing yards on Sunday until the very end of the game when Rachaad White busted a 33-yarder late in the fourth quarter. The Commanders finished with 135 rushing yards.

And that means the Eagles have now given up at least 100 rushing yards in all eight games to begin this season. That's their longest such streak to begin a season since 1968.

"Inconsistent. Inconsistent," defensive coordinator Vic Fangio said about his run defense after eight games. "Thought it was OK to borderline good [on Sunday]. Then at the end of the game, we gave up the 33 yarder."

Overall, the Eagles are now 28th in run defense this season, allowing 131.9 yards per game. That's obviously not good enough. The Eagles have also struggling rushing the passer but many think these two issues are connected. Sometimes you have to earn the right to rush the passer and that starts by stopping the run.

Jordan Davis is the lead run-stuffer in this defense and after signing a $78 million extension this offseason, Davis isn't living up to expectations.

"He's been not quite as good as he had been in the past, these last couple weeks," Fangio said. "Think he's got a little problem with his hand there, but he can be better, but we love Jordan and he'll get there."

#2851. NFC North has 17-3 record against non-NFC North teams

The NFC North is the best division in the NFL. As evidenced by how the teams of the NFC North have performed against the rest of the league.

The Vikings, Bears, Lions, and Packers have a combined record of 17-3 against non-NFC North teams. Via NBC Sports research, the ensuing 85 percent success rate is the highest by any division in the NFL since the 1970 merger.

In theory, all four teams of the NFC North could make the playoffs. To do that, however, each would have to finish higher in the wild-card pecking order than the Rams and the Seahawks (assuming the 49ers win the NFC West).

This week, two teams of the NFC North have an easier path to victory than the others; the Bears host the Buccaneers and the Lions visit the Dolphins.

For the Packers (who play the Patriots in New England) and the Vikings (who play the Bills on Monday night), it will be harder to keep pushing 17-3 to 21-3.

#2849. Reports: Formula One has Tuesday deadline to switch race to Imola

A decision on a possible Formula One season finale at Italy's Imola track rather than in the Middle East must be made by Tuesday, motorsport media reported on Saturday.

The season is meant to conclude with races in Qatar on November 29 and Abu Dhabi a week later. But conflict and tensions in the Middle East mean F1 is mulling having just one race in Imola instead, although it has publicly backed Qatar and Abu Dhabi.

Organizers in the Italian region of Emilia-Romagna need to know imminently if they should step in as a replacement. After Tuesday, there would not be enough time to prepare.

Haas team boss Ayao Komatsu also confirmed Tuesday's deadline, according to broadcaster ESPN.

Imola as a plan B would be held in December. It would mean a home race for new world champion Kimi Antonelli of Mercedes.

If time runs out for Imola and an escalation makes the grands prix in Qatar and Abu Dhabi impossible for security reasons, the season could even end on November 21 in Las Vegas.

But according to ESPN, Formula One has promised teams at least 22 races this season. A Las Vegas finale would be race number 21.

#2848. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2847. ‘There's no magic potion:' Vic Fangio pinpoints the issue with Jonathan Greenard

'There's no magic potion:' Vic Fangio pinpoints the issue with Jonathan Greenard originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jonathan Greenard, the Eagles' $98 million free agent edge rusher, doesn't have much to show for his first two months as an Eagle.

Two tackles, one quarterback hit and one forced fumble (that the Eagles didn't recover) to show in 118 snaps over parts of five games.

"What I see is a guy that has had very, very little practice," Fangio said Tuesday. "I mean, when I say very little, minuscule.

"He missed all of training camp, missed the first two weeks of the season, no practice, started practicing, was on a pitch count, played 19 plays, was going to increase the next week, got hurt early in that game, didn't finish that game, didn't play the next game, and he's been limited in practice."

Greenard hasn't had a full week of practice yet. The Eagles, 2-6 with a six-game losing streak, open the practice week on Wednesday for a home game Sunday against the Giants.

Greenard has had exactly six days of full practice so far this year – the Friday before the Bears game, all three days leading up to the Rams, the Friday leading up to the Panthers and the Friday before the game Sunday night in Washington.

"You know, contrary to some people's popular belief, practice is important," Fangio said. "And he has had next to none."

Greenard missed all of training camp and the first two games with a pectoral injury, played 18 snaps against the Bears and 19 against the Rams, then missed the Jaguars game with an ankle injury. He's played 24, 29 and 28 snaps the last three weeks with modest results.

Prior to the Cowboys game, Greenard was limited on Thursday and missed practice Friday and Saturday. Last week, he was limited at practice on Wednesday and Thursday and full on Friday.

When he has played, Greenard hasn't been close to the player the Eagles envisioned. This is a guy who had 24 ½ sacks in 2023 and 2024 and made a Pro Bowl in 2024. His $24.5 million average annual salary is 12th-highest among all NFL edge rushers.

"Practice," Fangio said. "You know, there's no pill, there's no magic potion. You can watch all the tape you want, you can sit in meetings all you want, you can do walkthroughs all you want. The guy's got to practice. Practice is important for everybody."

The Eagles could sure use some pass pressure.

Nolan Smith has four sacks but he's the Eagles' only edge rusher with any sacks. Overall, the Eagles have just 10 sacks, 4th-fewest in the league and the fewest in franchise history through eight games.

You always take Pro Football Focus grades with a grain of salt, but out of 118 edge rushers who've played at least 100 snaps his 57.1 overall grade ranks 93rdand his 65.1 pressure grade ranks 77th.

According to Stathead analytics, Greenard has no quarterback hurries, and he's played the 6th-most snaps among all edge rushers who have no sacks or hurries.

"Yeah, he's just not into a groove," Fangio said. "He's not comfortable in our system totally and what we want him to do. And it's not because he doesn't know or doesn't want to. It's just he hasn't executed it. Walk-throughs are an assignment check. It's not execution.

"I don't know that I've had a guy that's had so little practice."

#2846. Drew Rosenhaus after Brandon Aiyuk's meeting with 49ers: "To be continued"

It has become abundantly clear that the NFL is delaying the reinstatement of 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk from the reserve/left squad list. It has also become abundantly clear that the 49ers hope to squeeze some cash out of Aiyuk before the league lets him back in.

It’s no surprise, then, that Friday’s sit-down between Aiyuk, agent Drew Rosenhaus, and the 49ers did not resolve the situation.

That seems to be precisely where things stand. And the question becomes whether Aiyuk will be willing to strike a deal with the 49ers in order to secure the ability to return to the NFL, with another team.

#2843. Saints to have "chip on our shoulder" after Browns' "chirping"

The Saints, like all teams, pay attention to the things said by their upcoming opponents.

Earlier this week, Browns quarterback Shedeur Sanders seemed to make some less-than-flattering comments about the New Orleans defense. The New Orleans defense heard it, loud and clear.

“We’re gonna come in with a chip on our shoulder, for sure,” Saints defensive end Carl Granderson said Friday. “You know, they been chirping a little bit. We heard what the quarterback . . . said. So, you know, we’re [going to] come in hot and ready to play. . . . It’s gonna be a good game, for sure. We’re gonna come out with the [win].”

Asked Wednesday by reporters whether he has had a chance to study the Saints defense and to explain what he has seen, Sanders flashed a broad smile.

“Yeah, I’ve seen a lot,” Sanders said. “When I smile, just know that means something. I’m maturing. I’m maturing. Like, I could have given a decent answer, but now I’m just going to smile whenever it’s just, like, some questions.”

The better approach for Sanders would have been to project a poker face, and to give an answer that did nothing to attract the attention of the Saints’ defense.

So while he says he’s maturing, the ultimate level of maturity would consist of providing the most vanilla answer possible, praising the Saints and explaining that it will be a major challenge to beat them.

Instead, a Browns player has provided the next opponent — for the second straight week — with a little extra incentive.
